PI, CV, and US correspond to plug-in, cross-validation, and undersmoothing respectively, and PI + AV corresponds to the plug-in bandwidth with the direct plug-in estimator of the asymptotic variance.

Figure
4
displays the empirical coverage of confidence intervals for the causal effect
\theta_{0}(a)-\theta_{0}(0.5)
based on the debiased estimator and the two variance estimators described following Theorem
2
. The confidence intervals based on the variance estimator using asymptotic independence (top row) are conservative when
a
is close to 0.5 because the finite-sample covariance between the estimators is positive when the distance between the evaluation points is small. When
a
is further from
0.5
, the confidence intervals have better coverage. The confidence intervals using the influence function-based variance estimator (
second
row) have much better coverage for all values of
a
, especially at large sample sizes, because the variance estimator captures some of the finite-sample covariance between the estimators.
For both approaches, the plug-in method is conservative when the outcome regression is misspecified.

Figure 4
:
Empirical coverage of 95% pointwise confidence intervals for
\theta_{0}(a)-\theta_{0}(0.5)
based on the debiased estimator. The intervals in the top row use the sum of two variance estimators; those in the bottom row use the influence function-based variance estimator.

Finally, Figure
5
displays the empirical coverage of the uniform confidence bands. For this simulation, we considered the augmented set of sample sizes
n\in\{500,750,1000,1250,1500,2000\}
. The plug-in bandwidth selection method exhibits slight undercoverage for sample sizes less than 1000, but generally performs well. However, both bandwidth selection methods based on cross-validation have serious undercoverage at sample sizes less than 1500. We conjecture that this undercoverage is a result of the bandwidth selected using LOOCV being smaller on average than that selected by the plug-in methodology. Smaller bandwidths result in a process with smaller correlation between points and whose supremum is stochastically larger. This results in a slower rate of convergence for the approximation in Theorem
3
. This further illustrates the benefit of permitting bandwidths to be selected at the optimal rate.

Figure 5
:
Empirical coverage of
95\%
uniform confidence bands based on the debiased estimator.

An important conclusion of the numerical studies is that the plug-in bandwidth selection method with
h=b
consistently demonstrates good coverage for all inferential tasks we examined. Additionally, the plug-in method yields smaller variance and narrower confidence intervals than the other debiased methods and the undersmoothed local linear estimator. Based on these findings, we recommend using the plug-in method with
h=b
for bandwidth selection.

The Supplementary Material contains additional results from the numerical study, including the results presented here for more sample sizes, median width of confidence intervals, and results when the nuisance estimators are based on parametric models.

5
Effect of fine particulate matter on cardiovascular mortality

Fine particulate matter is a common air pollutant, and its concentration in the United States is regulated by the Environmental Protection Agency under the Clean Air Act. Numerous scientific studies have reported an association between exposure to air pollution and adverse health outcomes. The reduction in the concentration of particulate matter in the atmosphere over the past several decades is considered one of the contributors to the declining cardiovascular mortality rate
(
Pope et al., 2002
;
Pope et al., 2009
;
Correia et al., 2013
;
Roth et al., 2017
;
Corrigan et al., 2018
)
.

Wyatt et al., 2020a
recently conducted an observational study to investigate the association between particulate matter less than 2.5 microns in diameter (PM
2.5
) and cardiovascular mortality rate. Socioeconomic factors are a potential confounding of this relationship because they impact both exposure to PM
2.5
and cardiovascular mortality. Using data recorded at the county level in the United States between 1990 and 2010, the authors found a positive association between PM
2.5
and cardiovascular mortality rate after adjusting for socioeconomic characteristics using regression models.

We used the publicly available data compiled by
Wyatt et al., 2020b
to estimate the covariate-adjusted relationship between PM
2.5
and cardiovascular mortality rate using the methods presented here. The data contains information about
n=2132
counties. Our exposure
A
was the county-level annual PM
2.5
(in
\mu
g/m
3
) averaged over twenty observations from 1990 to 2010, as measured by US Environmental Protection Agency’s Community Multi-scale Air Quality modeling system
(
Gan et al., 2015
)
. Our outcome
Y
was county-level cardiovascular mortality rate (CMR, deaths/100,000 people) in 2010, as measured by the National Center for Health Statistics. Our covariates
W
consisted of county-level socioeconomic factors based on 1990 and 2000 census data: population, households below the poverty level, proportion of female-headed households with dependent children, vacant housing units, owner-occupied housing units, median household income, high education rate, unemployment rate, and density of medical facilities. We note that there are very likely to be violations of the stable unit treatment value assumption in this data since air pollution in one county could impact the health of residents in neighboring counties. In addition, the data are unlikely to be independent. It would be of interest in future research to extend the methods presented here to deal with treatment spillover and dependent data.

We estimated the outcome regression using SuperLearner
(
van der Laan et al., 2007
)
with a library consisting of generalized linear models, multivariate adaptive regression splines, generalized additive models, and regression trees. We estimated the conditional density using the version of SuperLearner developed by
Díaz Muñoz and van der Laan, 2011
with the same library. We used the Epanechnikov kernel and selected the bandwidth
h
using the plug-in method discussed in Section
2.5
and set
b=h
. We focus on values of
PM
2.5
between
2.5\mu g/m^{3}
and
11.5\mu g/m^{3}
, which approximately corresponds to the 0.01 and 0.99 quantiles
of the marginal empirical distribution of PM
2.5
, respectively.

Figure 6
:
Estimated covariate-adjusted relationship between the concentration of fine particulate matter (PM
2.5
) in the atmosphere and cardiovascular mortality rate at the county level. The regression function is adjusted for socioeconomic factors.
The figure shows 95% pointwise confidence intervals as vertical lines and a 95% uniform confidence band as dashed lines.
The right panel displays the estimated regression function based on the local linear estimator.

Figure
6
displays the estimated covariate-adjusted regression function using our debiased method.
The result based on the local linear estimator with the plug-in bandwidth selection is also provided for comparison purposes.
Pointwise 95% confidence intervals are displayed using vertical lines, and a 95% uniform confidence band is displayed using dashed lines. The adjusted expected CMR appears to be monotone increasing as a function of PM
2.5
, and its rate of increase is fastest for PM
2.5
values between roughly 6 and 8
\mu g/m^{3}
. Our method shows larger uncertainty for PM
2.5
levels below 4
\mu g/m^{3}
and above 9
\mu g/m^{3}
due to the low density of PM
2.5
. It is unclear if average CMR increases or plateaus over these regions. We estimate that increasing PM
2.5
from 6 to 8
\mu g/m^{3}
corresponds to an increase of
10.3 in expected CMR after adjusting for socioeconomic factors (95% CI: 7.6–12.9)
, and increasing PM
2.5
from 5 to 9
\mu g/m^{3}
corresponds to an increase of
17.5 in expected CMR (95% CI: 14.8–20.2)
. Our conclusions generally agree with those reported in
Wyatt et al., 2020a
, but our flexible approach to estimation and principled approach to inference ensure that the conclusions are not the result of statistical bias.
We also note that in this application, it may be sensible to assume the dose-response function is monotone increasing, so the methods of
Westling et al., 2020
could be used instead.

6
Concluding remarks

In this article, we studied nonparametric inference for a covariate-adjusted regression function. This problem has wide applications in observational studies when the exposure of interest is continuous. In particular, under appropriate causal assumptions, the covariate-adjusted regression function corresponds to the average counterfactual outcome had all units been assigned to the same fixed exposure level. We presented conditions under which our proposed procedures yield valid pointwise and uniform inference. Our conditions do not require undersmoothing and permit the use of data-adaptive estimators for nuisance functions, and our results do not require more smoothness conditions than the original local linear estimator.

Our method requires the choice of two tuning parameters: the bandwidth
h
of the original local linear estimator and the bandwidth
b
of the bias correction. We considered several methods of selecting these tuning parameter and compared them in numerical studies. In practice, we suggest choosing
h
using the plug-in method and setting
b=h
because this method had the best overall confidence interval and band coverage rates while also maintaining the lowest variance and narrowest median confidence interval length among the debiased methods.

There are several natural extensions to our work. Cross-fitting the nuisance estimators would remove the empirical process conditions. Debiasing higher-order local polynomial estimators would yield faster rates of convergence under stronger smoothness assumptions. Debiased estimators of derivatives of the parameter could be obtained using similar methodology. Debiasing a higher-order corrected estimator would yield weaker assumptions for the rates of convergence of the nuisance estimators
(
Bonvini and Kennedy, 2022
)
. Alternative procedures for targeting the smoothed parameter, such as targeted minimum loss-based estimation
(
van der Laan and Rose, 2011
)
could yield improved finite-sample or asymptotic properties of the methods. Finally, twicing kernels
(
Newey et al., 2004
;
Zhang and Xia, 2012
)
may be an alternative approach to bias correction.

Supplement A
Guide to notation

We recall the data unit is
O:=(Y,A,W)
, and takes value in the sample space
\mathcal{Y}\times\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W}
, where
\mathcal{Y}\subseteq\mathbb{R}
,
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, and
\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We observe
n
IID replicates of this data unit, which we call
O_{1},\dotsc,O_{n}
, and we define the unknown distribution of
O_{i}
as
P_{0}
. We index objects and functions by
P
when they depend on a distribution
P
generating
O
. We index by
0
when
P=P_{0}
. We index by
n
when the object is random depending on the sample
O_{1},\dotsc,O_{n}
. Finally, we index by
\infty
when the object is a limit of an object indexed by
n
that may or may not be equal to its true counterpart depending on
P_{0}
.

•

F_{P}(a):=P(A\leq a)
and
f_{P}(a):=\frac{\partial}{\partial a}F_{P}(a)
is the marginal CDF and PDF of
A
under
P

•

Q_{P}(\mathcal{X}):=P(W\in\mathcal{X})
is the marginal distribution of
W
under
P

•

\mu_{P}(a,w):=E_{P}(Y\mid A=a,W=w)
as the outcome regression function under
P

•

\theta_{P}(a):=E_{P}[\mu_{P}(a,W)]=\int\mu_{P}(a,w)\,dQ_{P}(w)
is the
G
-computed regression function

•

g_{P}(a,w):=\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial a}P(A\leq a\mid W=w)\right]/f_{P}(a)
is the standardized conditional density of
A
given
W=w

•

Pf:=\int f(o)\,dP(o)

•

\|\beta\|_{P,q}:=(P|\beta|^{q})^{1/q}

•

•

\mathbb{P}_{n}
is the empirical probability measure corresponding to
O_{1},\dotsc,O_{n}

•

•

•

for

&\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\times e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\xi_{n})\end{aligned}

•

\begin{aligned} \phi^{*}_{P,h,b,a_{0}}(y,a,w)&:=\Gamma_{P,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\xi_{P}(y,a,w)-\gamma_{P,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt+\int\Gamma_{P,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\left\{\mu_{P}(\bar{a},w)-\int\mu_{P}(\bar{a},\bar{w})\,dQ_{P}(\bar{w})\right\}\,dF_{P}(\bar{a})\end{aligned}

•

\begin{aligned} \phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}(y,a,w)&:=\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\xi_{\infty}(y,a,w)-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt+\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\left\{\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},w)-\int\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},\bar{w})\,dQ_{0}(\bar{w})\right\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\end{aligned}

•

\begin{aligned} \phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}(y,a,w)&:=\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\xi_{n}(y,a,w)-\gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt+\int\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\left\{\mu_{n}(\bar{a},w)-\int\mu_{n}(\bar{a},\bar{w})\,dQ_{n}(\bar{w})\right\}\,dF_{n}(\bar{a})\end{aligned}

•

\tau_{n}:=h_{n}/b_{n}

•

\sigma_{0}^{2}(a):=E_{0}\left[\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)-\theta_{0}(A)\right\}^{2}\mid A=a\right]

•

\sigma_{\infty,h,b}^{2}(a_{0}):=hP_{0}\left(\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}\right)^{2}
.

•

\sigma_{n,h,b}^{2}(a_{0}):=h\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}\right)^{2}
.

•

\omega_{n}:=\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\inf_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}|a_{0}-a_{n}|
.

Throughout, we use
\lesssim
to mean “less than up to a constant not depending on
n
,
h
, or
b
.

Supplement B
Conditions

Here, we re-state the conditions we use in our asymptotic results for convenience. These are the same conditions as stated in the main text.

(A1)

The kernel
K
is a mean-zero, symmetric, nonnegative, and Lipschitz continuous density function with support contained in
[-1,1]
. Additionally,
K
belongs to the linear span of the functions whose subgraph can be represented as a finite number of Boolean operations among sets of the form
\{(s,u)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}:p(s,u)\leq\varphi(u)\}
where
p
is a polynomial and
\varphi
is an arbitrary real function.

(A2)

As
n\longrightarrow\infty
, the bandwidths
h=h_{n}
and
b=b_{n}
satisfy
h_{n}\longrightarrow 0
,
nh_{n}\longrightarrow\infty
,
b_{n}\longrightarrow 0
, and
\tau_{n}:=h_{n}/b_{n}\longrightarrow\tau\in[0,\infty)
.

We define the following pseudo-distance for any
P_{0}
-square integrable functions
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}:\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
,
\mathcal{A}_{0}\subset\mathcal{A}
, and
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W}
:

\displaystyle d(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2};\mathcal{A}_{0},\mathcal{S}):=\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\{E_{0}[I_{\mathcal{S}}(a,W)\{\gamma_{1}(a,W)-\gamma_{2}(a,W)\}^{2}]\right\}^{1/2}

We also define
B_{\varepsilon}(a_{0})
as the closed ball of radius
\varepsilon
centered at
a_{0}
.

(A3)

There exist classes of functions
\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
and
\mathcal{F}_{g}
such that almost surely for all
n
large enough,
\mu_{0},\mu_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
,
g_{0},g_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{g}
, and for some constants
C_{j}\in(0,\infty)
,
V_{\mu}\in(0,1)
, and
V_{g}\in(0,2)
:

(a)

\|\mu\|_{\infty}\leq C_{1}
for all
\mu\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
, and
\|1/g\|_{\infty}\leq C_{2}
and
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq C_{3}
for all
g\in\mathcal{F}_{g}
;

(b)

\sup_{Q}\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F}_{\mu},L_{2}(Q))\leq C_{4}\varepsilon^{-V_{\mu}}
and
\sup_{Q}\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F}_{g},L_{2}(Q))\leq C_{5}\varepsilon^{-V_{g}}
for all
\varepsilon>0
.

(A4)

There exist
\mu_{\infty}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
and
g_{\infty}\in\mathcal{F}_{g}
,
\delta_{1}>0
, and subsets
\mathcal{S}_{1},\mathcal{S}_{2}
and
\mathcal{S}_{3}
of
B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})\times\mathcal{W}
such that
\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2}\cup\mathcal{S}_{3}=B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})\times\mathcal{W}
and:

(a)

\mu_{\infty}(a,w)=\mu_{0}(a,w)
for all
(a,w)\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{3}
and
g_{\infty}(a,w)=g_{0}(a,w)
for all
(a,w)\in\mathcal{S}_{2}\cup\mathcal{S}_{3}
;

(b)

d(\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{1})=o_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right)
, and
d(g_{n},g_{\infty};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{1})=o_{p}(1)
;

(c)

d(g_{n},g_{\infty};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{2})\}=o_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right)
, and
d(\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{2})=o_{p}(1)
;

(d)

d(\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{3})d(g_{n},g_{\infty};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{3})=o_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right)
.

(A5)

It holds that:

(a)

\theta_{0}
is twice continuously differentiable on
B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
;

(b)

f_{0}
is positive and Lipschitz continuous on
B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
;

(c)

there exist
\delta_{2}>0
and
C_{6}<\infty
such that
E_{0}[|Y|^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a,W=w]\leq C_{6}
for all
a\in B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
and
P_{0}
-almost every
w
and
E_{0}[|Y|^{4}]<\infty
; and

(d)

a\mapsto\sigma_{0}^{2}(a):=E_{0}\left[\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)-\theta_{0}(A)\}^{2}\mid A=a\right]
is bounded and continuous on
B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
, where
\xi_{\infty}:=\xi_{\mu_{\infty},g_{\infty},Q_{0}}
is the limiting pseudo-outcome.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{0}
be a compact subset of
\mathcal{A}
over which we wish to perform uniform inference. For
\delta>0
define
\mathcal{A}_{\delta}
as the
\delta
-enlargement of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
; that is, the set of
a\in\mathbb{R}
such that there exists
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
with
|a-a_{0}|\leq\delta
. We then assume there exists
\delta_{3}>0
such that the following hold.

(A6)

The constant
V:=\max\{V_{\mu},V_{g}\}
, for
V_{\mu}
and
V_{g}
defined in
(A3)
and the bandwidth
h
satisfies
n\left[h/(\log n)\right]^{\frac{2+V}{2-V}}\longrightarrow\infty
and
nh^{3}\longrightarrow\infty
.

(A7)

There exist
\mu_{\infty}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
,
g_{\infty}\in\mathcal{F}_{g}
,
\delta_{3}>0
and subsets
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}\times\mathcal{W}
such that
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\prime}=\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}\times\mathcal{W}
and:

(a)

\mu_{\infty}(a,w)=\mu_{0}(a,w)
for all
(a,w)\in\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\prime}
and
g_{\infty}(a,w)=g_{0}(a,w)
for all
(a,w)\in\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\prime}
;

(b)

d(\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty};\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}},\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime})=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
;

(c)

d(g_{n},g_{\infty};\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}},\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime})\}=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
;

(d)

d(\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty};\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}},\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\prime})d(g_{n},g_{\infty};\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}},\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\prime})=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
.

(e)

d(\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty};\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}},\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W})
and
d(g_{n},g_{\infty};\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}},\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W})
are both
o_{p}\left(h^{\frac{V}{2(2-V)}}\{\log n\}^{-\frac{1}{2-V}}\right)

(A8)

It holds that:

(a)

\theta_{0}
is twice continuously differentiable with Hölder-continuous second derivative on
\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}
;

(b)

f_{0}
is Lipschitz continuous and bounded away from 0 and
\infty
on
\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}
;

(c)

|Y|
is
P_{0}
-almost surely bounded; and

(d)

a\mapsto E_{0}\left[\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)-\theta_{0}(A)\}^{2}\mid A=a\right]
is continuous on
\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}
.

Supplement C
Efficient influence function of the local parameter

Proof of Lemma 1.

We let
\{P_{\varepsilon}:|\varepsilon|\leq\delta\}
be a one-dimensional quadratic mean differentiable path contained in the model
\mathcal{M}
such that
P_{\epsilon=0}=P_{0}
. We let
(y,a,w)\mapsto\dot{\ell}_{0}(y,a,w)
be the score function of the path at
\varepsilon=0
, which necessarily satisfies
P_{0}\dot{\ell}_{0}=0
and
P_{0}\dot{\ell}^{2}_{0}<\infty
. We define the marginal score functions
\dot{\ell}_{0}(a,w):=E_{0}[\dot{\ell}_{0}\mid A=a,W=w]
,
\dot{\ell}_{0}(a):=E_{0}[\dot{\ell}_{0}\mid A=a]
, and
\dot{\ell}_{0}(w):=E[\dot{\ell}_{0}\mid W=w]
, which all have mean zero under
P_{0}
by the tower property. We also define the conditional score function
\dot{\ell}_{0}(y\mid a,w):=\dot{\ell}_{0}(y,a,w)-\dot{\ell}(a,w)
, which satisfies
E_{0}[\dot{\ell}_{0}(Y\mid A,W)\mid A=a,W=w]=0
for
P_{0}
-almost every
(a,w)
. For convenience, we denote objects depending on
P_{\varepsilon}
with a subscript
\varepsilon
.

To demonstrate that
\phi^{*}_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
is the nonparametric efficient influence function of
\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})=\theta_{0}^{DB}(a_{0})
, we show that

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\theta_{\varepsilon,h,b}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=P_{0}(\phi^{*}_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\dot{\ell}_{0})

for each fixed
h,b>0
and
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}
. We define

\displaystyle\theta_{P,h}(a_{0})

\displaystyle:=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{P,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{P}\right)\text{ and}

\displaystyle\theta_{P,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})

\displaystyle:=2b^{-2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{P,b,a_{0},2}P\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{P}\right),

so that
\theta_{P,h,b}(a_{0})
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\theta_{P,h,b}(a_{0})=\theta_{P,h}(a_{0})-\tfrac{1}{2}c_{P,h,a_{0},2}h^{2}\theta^{\prime\prime}_{P,b}(a_{0}).

Therefore,

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\theta_{\varepsilon,h,b}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\theta_{\varepsilon,h}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}-\tfrac{1}{2}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}c_{\varepsilon,h,a_{0},2}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}h^{2}\theta^{\prime\prime}_{P,b}(a_{0})-\tfrac{1}{2}c_{P,h,a_{0},2}h^{2}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\theta^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon,b}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

We now provide the derivation of
\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\theta_{\varepsilon,h}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}
. We first have by the product rule that

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\theta_{\varepsilon,h}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{\varepsilon,h,a_{0},1}P_{\varepsilon}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{\varepsilon,h,a_{0},1}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)+e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{\varepsilon}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}.

For the first term, by the definition of
\mathbf{D}_{P,h,a_{0}}
as the mean of a fixed and uniformly bounded function and the chain rule, we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{\varepsilon,h,a_{0},1}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=-e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\mathbf{D}_{\varepsilon,h,a_{0},1}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}

\displaystyle=-e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}w^{T}_{h,a_{0},1}\dot{\ell}_{0}\right)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}.

Similarly, for the second term, we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{\varepsilon}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\dot{\ell}_{0}\right).

For the third term, we have by definition of
\theta_{P}

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\iint y\,dP_{\varepsilon}(y\mid a,w)\,dQ_{\varepsilon}(w)\,dF_{0}(a)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\iint y\dot{\ell}_{0}(y\mid a,w)\,dP_{0}(y\mid a,w)\,dQ_{0}(w)\,dF_{0}(a)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\iint y\,dP_{0}(y\mid a,w)\dot{\ell}_{0}(w)\,dQ_{0}(w)\,dF_{0}(a).

By properties of score functions and since
g_{0}(a,w)>0
for
F_{0}
-a.e.
a
such that
|a-a_{0}|\leq h
and
Q_{0}
-a.e.
w
, the first term equals

\displaystyle\iiint w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\frac{y}{g_{0}(a,w)}\dot{\ell}_{0}(y\mid a,w)\,dP_{0}(y,a,w)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\iiint w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\frac{y-\mu_{0}(a,w)}{g_{0}(a,w)}\dot{\ell}_{0}(y,a,w)\,dP_{0}(y,a,w).

The second term equals

\displaystyle\iint w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{0}(a,w)\,dF_{0}(a)\dot{\ell}_{0}(w)\,dQ_{0}(w)

\displaystyle=\int\eta_{0}(w)\dot{\ell}_{0}(w)\,dQ_{0}(w)

\displaystyle=\iiint\eta_{0}(w)\dot{\ell}_{0}(y,a,w)\,dP_{0}(y,a,w),

where
\eta_{0}(w):=\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{0}(a,w)\,dF_{0}(a)
. Putting it together, we have that
\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}\theta_{\varepsilon,h}(a_{0})\right|_{\varepsilon=0}
equals

\displaystyle-e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}w^{T}_{h,a_{0},1}\dot{\ell}_{0}\right)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\dot{\ell}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\iiint\left\{\left[w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\left\{\frac{y-\mu_{0}(a,w)}{g_{0}(a,w)}\right\}+\eta_{0}(w)\right]\dot{\ell}_{0}(y,a,w)\right\}\,dP_{0}(y,a,w)

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\iiint\left\{\left[w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\left\{\frac{y-\mu_{0}(a,w)}{g_{0}(a,w)}+\theta_{0}(a)-\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}(a)\right\}+\eta_{0}(w)\right]\dot{\ell}_{0}(y,a,w)\right\}\,dP_{0}(y,a,w)

where
\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}(a):=w^{T}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)
.
Therefore, the uncentered influence function of
\theta_{P,h}(a_{0})
at
P=P_{0}
is

\phi_{0,h,a_{0}}(y,a,w):=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\left[w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\left\{\frac{y-\mu_{0}(a,w)}{g_{0}(a,w)}+\theta_{0}(a)-\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}(a)\right\}+\eta_{0}(w)\right]

Noting that

P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}\right)=P_{0}\eta_{0}=P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right),

the mean of
\phi_{0,h,a_{0}}
is

\displaystyle P_{0}\phi_{0,h,a_{0}}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right).

Thus, the centered influence function of
\theta_{P,h}
at
P_{0}
is

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{0,h,a_{0}}(y,a,w)

\displaystyle=\phi_{0,h,a_{0}}(y,a,w)-P_{0}\phi_{0,h,a_{0}}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\left\{\frac{y-\mu_{0}(a,w)}{g_{0}(a,w)}+\theta_{0}(a)-\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}(a)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\{\mu_{0}(a,w)-\theta_{0}(a)\}\,dF_{0}(a)

By a similar argument, the influence function of
\theta_{P,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})
at
P_{0}
is

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{0,b,a_{0}}(y,a,w)

\displaystyle=2b^{-2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\left\{\frac{y-\mu_{0}(a,w)}{g_{0}(a,w)}+\theta_{0}(a)-\gamma_{0,b,a_{0}}(a)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+2b^{-2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\int w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\{\mu_{0}(a,w)-\theta_{0}(a)\}\,dF_{0}(a)

where
\gamma_{0,b,a_{0}}(a):=w^{T}_{b,a_{0},2}(a)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)
. We next have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}c_{\varepsilon,h,a_{0},2}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{\varepsilon,h,a_{0},1}P_{\varepsilon}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{\varepsilon,h,a_{0},1}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}P_{0}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)+e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\varepsilon}P_{\varepsilon}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=-e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}w^{T}_{h,a_{0},1}\dot{\ell}_{0}\right)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)+e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\dot{\ell}_{0}\right).

Hence, the uncentered influence function of
c_{P,h,a_{0},2}
is

\displaystyle\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}^{c}(a)

\displaystyle=-e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)w^{T}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)+e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\left[\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)-w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)w^{T}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\right]K_{h,a_{0}}(a).

This function has mean zero, so it is the nonparametric efficient influence function of
c_{P,h,a_{0},2}
at
P=P_{0}
.

Putting the three pieces together, we have

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(y,a,w)=\phi^{*}_{0,h,a_{0}}(y,a,w)-\tfrac{1}{2}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}h^{2}\phi^{*}_{0,b,a_{0}}(y,a,w)-\tfrac{1}{2}\gamma_{0,h,a_{0}}^{c}(a)h^{2}\theta_{0,b}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\left\{\frac{y-\mu_{0}(a,w)}{g_{0}(a,w)}+\theta_{0}(a)\right\}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)+\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\{\mu_{0}(a,w)-\theta_{0}(a)\}\,dF_{0}(a).

∎

Supplement D
Decomposition of the estimator

In this section, we present a decomposition of the estimator into six remainder terms. Before we present the decomposition, we provide a supporting Lemma showing that the pseudo-outcome function possesses a double robustness property under
(A4)
(a).

Lemma 2
.

If
(A4)
(a) holds, then

\displaystyle\theta_{0}(a)=E_{0}\left\{\frac{Y-\mu_{\infty}(A,W)}{g_{\infty}(A,W)}+\int\mu_{\infty}(A,w)\,dQ_{0}(w)\,\Big|\,A=a\right\}

for
F_{0}
-almost every
a\in B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
.

Proof of Lemma
2
.

By the tower property and since
dP_{0}(w\mid A=a)=g_{0}(a,w)\,dQ_{0}(w)
, we have for any
a
in the support of
A
that

\displaystyle E_{0}\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)\mid A=a\right\}

\displaystyle=E_{0}\left\{\frac{Y-\mu_{\infty}(A,W)}{g_{\infty}(A,W)}+\int\mu_{\infty}(A,w)\,dQ_{0}(w)\,\Big|\,A=a\right\}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mu_{0}(a,w)-\mu_{\infty}(a,w)}{g_{\infty}(a_{0},w)}g_{0}(a,w)\,dQ_{0}(w)+\int\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\,dQ_{0}(w)

\displaystyle=\int\{\mu_{0}(a,w)-\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\}\left\{\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{\infty}(a,w)}-1\right\}\,dQ_{0}(w)+\int\mu_{0}(a,w)\,dQ_{0}(w)

\displaystyle=\int\{\mu_{0}(a,w)-\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\}\left\{\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{\infty}(a,w)}-1\right\}\,dQ_{0}(w)+\theta_{0}(a).

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})}\left|E_{0}\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)\mid A=a\right\}-\theta_{0}(a)\right|\,dF_{0}(a)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\int_{B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})}\left|\int\{\mu_{0}(a,w)-\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\}\left\{\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{\infty}(a,w)}-1\right\}\,dQ_{0}(w)\right|\,dF_{0}(a)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\iint_{B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})\times\mathcal{W}}\left|\{\mu_{0}(a,w)-\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\}\left\{\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{\infty}(a,w)}-1\right\}\right|\,d(F_{0}\times Q_{0})(a,w)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}\left|\{\mu_{0}(a,w)-\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\}\left\{\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{\infty}(a,w)}-1\right\}\right|\,d(F_{0}\times Q_{0})(a,w).

By
(A4)
(a), at least one of the two terms inside the integral is zero for each of the three integrals over
\mathcal{S}_{1}
,
\mathcal{S}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{3}
. Hence, the above expression is zero, and the claim follows.
∎

We now provide the decomposition of the estimator that we will use throughout our results. The decomposition involves a leading empirical mean,
\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}
, which drives the first-order asymptotic behavior of the estimator, and six remainder terms. We separate the remainder into six terms because the methods of controlling these remainders are conceptually distinct, as we will see in the ensuing results.

Lemma 3
.

If
(A1)
and
(A4)
(a) hold, then the following expansion of the estimator holds for all
h\in(0,\delta_{1})
:

\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})=\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}+\sum_{j=1}^{6}\mathrel{R}_{n,h,b,a_{0},j},

where

\displaystyle\mathrel{R}_{n,h,b,a_{0},1}

\displaystyle:=\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0}),

\displaystyle\mathrel{R}_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}

\displaystyle:=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left[\left\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\left(\psi_{n}+\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}\right)+\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left.-\left\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\left(\psi_{\infty}+\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)+\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu_{\infty}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right],

\displaystyle\mathrel{R}_{n,h,b,a_{0},3}

\displaystyle:=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left\{\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\left(\psi_{n}+\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}\right)+\int\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathrel{R}_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}

\displaystyle:=\iint\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}\{\mu_{n}-\mu_{0}\}\left\{1-\frac{g_{0}}{g_{n}}\right\}\,dF_{0}\,dQ_{0},

\displaystyle\mathrel{R}_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}

\displaystyle:=\iint\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{n}(a,w)\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})(w)\,d(F_{0}-F_{n})(a),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\mathrel{R}_{n,h,b,a_{0},6}

\displaystyle:=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\left(\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\right)\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\right)P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{1,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\left(\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}\right)\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\right)P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\tau_{n}^{2}e_{1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\right)(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\tau_{n}^{2}e_{1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\right)\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\left(\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\right)P_{0}(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\tau_{n}^{2}(c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2})e_{3}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\right)P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

Proof of Lemma
3
.

It is straightforward to see that
P_{0}\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}=P_{0}\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)
.
Since
(A4)
(a) holds by assumption, Lemma
2
implies that
E_{0}\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)\mid A=a\right\}=\theta_{0}(a)
for
F_{0}
-almost every
a\in B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
, and since
(A1)
holds, for all
h\leq\delta_{1}
,
\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)=0
for
a
such that
|a-a_{0}|>\delta_{1}
. Thus,

P_{0}\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\xi_{\infty}\right)=E_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)E_{0}\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)\mid A\right\}\right]=E_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)\theta_{0}(A)\right]=P_{0}\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle P_{0}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\xi_{\infty}\right)-P_{0}\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}+\iint\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu_{\infty}\,dF_{0}\,dQ_{0}-\iint\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu_{\infty}\,dF_{0}\,dQ_{0}

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)-P_{0}\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)=0.

We now define

\displaystyle\gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}(a)

\displaystyle:=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)w_{h,a_{0},1}^{T}(a)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-e_{3}^{T}c_{n,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{0},3}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)w_{b,a_{0},2}^{T}(a)\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\tau_{n}^{2}e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\left[\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)-w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)w^{T}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\right]K_{h,a_{0}}(a)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\times e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}),

where
\theta_{n}:a\mapsto\int\mu_{n}(a,w)\,dQ_{n}(w)
, and we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}w_{h,a_{0},1}^{T}\right)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-e_{3}^{T}c_{n,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}w_{b,a_{0},2}^{T}\right)\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\tau_{n}^{2}e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\left[\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)-\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}w^{T}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\times e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{n})

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-e_{3}^{T}c_{n,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\tau_{n}^{2}e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\left[\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)-\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\right]e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{n})

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{n}\right)-e_{3}^{T}c_{n,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{n}-e_{3}^{T}c_{n,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}\right).

We then define the plug-in influence function estimator
\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}
as

\displaystyle\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}(y,a,w)

\displaystyle:=\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\xi_{n}(y,a,w)-\gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}(a)+\int\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\left\{\mu_{n}(\bar{a},w)-\int\mu_{n}(\bar{a},\bar{w})\,dQ_{n}(\bar{w})\right\}\,dF_{n}(\bar{a}),

and we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}\xi_{n}\right)-\mathbb{P}_{n}\gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}+\iint\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{n}\,dQ_{n}-\iint\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dQ_{n}\,dF_{n}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(\xi_{n}-\theta_{n})=\mathbb{P}_{n}\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}\psi_{n}=\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\mathbb{P}_{n}\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}.

Therefore,
\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})=\mathbb{P}_{n}\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}+\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}
, which establishes the one-step representation of the debiased estimator. By adding and subtracting terms and using the derivations above, we can now write

\displaystyle\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left(\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}-\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}\right)+\left\{\mathbb{P}_{n}\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}-\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})+P_{0}\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left\{\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})\right\}.

We note that the last summand in braces equals
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},1}
. This is a standard first-order expansion of the estimator
\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})
of the pathwise differentiable parameter
\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})
for fixed
h
and
b
, though our analysis in subsequent results will consider the case where
h
and
b
go to zero as
n
grows.

We now enter a calculation showing that the second and third summands above equal
\sum_{j=2}^{6}R_{n,h,b,a_{0},j}
. For convenience, in this derivation we omit the subscripts
h
,
b
, and
a_{0}
when it is clear. We have

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left(\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}-\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}\right)+\left\{\mathbb{P}_{n}\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{n}-\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})+P_{0}\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{\circ}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left(\left\{\Gamma_{n}\psi_{n}+\Gamma_{n}\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{n}-\gamma_{n}^{\circ}+\int\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\,dF_{n}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left.-\left\{\Gamma_{0}\psi_{\infty}+\Gamma_{0}\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}-\gamma_{0}+\int\Gamma_{0}\mu_{\infty}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\left\{\mathbb{P}_{n}(\Gamma_{n}\theta_{n})-P_{0}(\Gamma_{0}\theta_{0})+P_{0}\left(\Gamma_{n}\psi_{n}+\Gamma_{n}\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{n}-\gamma_{n}^{\circ}+\int\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\,dF_{n}-\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\,dF_{n}\,dQ_{n}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\Gamma_{n}\psi_{n}-\Gamma_{0}\psi_{\infty})+\left\{-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\gamma_{n}^{\circ}+\mathbb{P}_{n}(\Gamma_{n}\theta_{n})-P_{0}\gamma_{n}^{\circ}\right\}+\left\{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\gamma_{0}+P_{0}(\Gamma_{n}\theta_{0})-P_{0}(\Gamma_{0}\theta_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\left\{dQ_{n}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})+dF_{n}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})+dQ_{n}\,dF_{0}+dF_{n}\,dQ_{0}-dF_{n}\,dQ_{n}-dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt-\iint\Gamma_{0}\mu_{\infty}\left\{dQ_{0}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})+dF_{0}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\left\{P_{0}(\Gamma_{n}\psi_{n})+\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}-P_{0}(\Gamma_{n}\theta_{0})\right\}

(9)

We note that we added and subtracted the terms
\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}
and
P_{0}\Gamma_{n}\theta_{0}
above. We address each of the summands in equation (
9
) in turn. First, we write

(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\Gamma_{n}\psi_{n}-\Gamma_{0}\psi_{\infty})=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left\{(\Gamma_{n}-\Gamma_{0})\psi_{n}\right\}+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left\{\Gamma_{0}(\psi_{n}-\psi_{\infty})\right\}.

Next, we note that since
\mathbb{P}_{n}\gamma_{n}^{\circ}=\mathbb{P}_{n}(\Gamma_{n}\theta_{n})
,

-(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\gamma_{n}^{\circ}+\mathbb{P}_{n}(\Gamma_{n}\theta_{n})-P_{0}\gamma_{n}^{\circ}=0.

Next, defining
\tilde{D}_{0,h}:=P_{0}(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})
and
\tilde{D}_{n,h}:=\mathbb{P}_{n}(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})
we have

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}+P_{0}(\Gamma_{n}\theta_{0})-P_{0}(\Gamma_{0}\theta_{0})

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,h}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h}\right)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-e_{3}^{T}c_{0,2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}(\mathbf{D}_{n,b}-\mathbf{D}_{0,b})\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+e_{1}^{T}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}\left(\tilde{D}_{n,h}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}\tilde{D}_{0,h}\right)e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+e_{1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}\right)P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)-e_{3}^{T}c_{0,2}\tau_{n}^{2}\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}\right)P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\tau_{n}^{2}(c_{n,2}-c_{0,2})e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)-\tau_{n}^{2}(c_{n,2}-c_{0,2})e_{3}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}\right)P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\left[\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}\mathbf{D}_{n,h}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h})+(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h})\right]P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-e_{3}^{T}c_{0,2}\tau_{n}^{2}\left[\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}\mathbf{D}_{n,b}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b})+(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b})\right]P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+e_{1}^{T}\tau_{n}^{2}\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}\tilde{D}_{n,h}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}\mathbf{D}_{n,h}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}\tilde{D}_{0,h}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h}^{-1}\tilde{D}_{n,h}+\mathbf{D}_{0,h}^{-1}\tilde{D}_{0,h}\right)e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\tau_{n}^{2}(c_{n,2}-c_{0,2})e_{3}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}\right)P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}(\mathbf{D}_{0,h}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h})(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h})P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-e_{3}^{T}c_{0,2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}(\mathbf{D}_{0,b}-\mathbf{D}_{n,b})(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b})P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-e_{1}^{T}\tau_{n}^{2}\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h}\right)\left[\left(\tilde{D}_{n,h}-\tilde{D}_{0,h}\right)+\mathbf{D}_{0,h}^{-1}\left(\mathbf{D}_{0,h}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h}\right)\tilde{D}_{0,h}\right]e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\tau_{n}^{2}(c_{n,2}-c_{0,2})e_{3}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b}\right)P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right),

which equals
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},6}
. We address the next two terms in equation (
9
) together:

\displaystyle\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}

\displaystyle\left\{dQ_{n}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})+dF_{n}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})+dQ_{n}\,dF_{0}+dF_{n}\,dQ_{0}-dF_{n}\,dQ_{n}-dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\iint\Gamma_{0}\mu_{\infty}\left\{dQ_{0}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})+dF_{0}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle=\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\left\{dQ_{n}\,dF_{n}-dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}\right\}-\iint\Gamma_{0}\mu_{n}\left\{dQ_{0}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})+dF_{0}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\iint\Gamma_{0}(\mu_{n}-\mu_{\infty})\left\{dQ_{0}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})+dF_{0}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle=\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\left\{dQ_{n}\,dF_{n}-dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}-dQ_{0}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})-dF_{0}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\iint\Gamma_{0}(\mu_{n}-\mu_{\infty})\left\{dQ_{0}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})+dF_{0}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\iint(\Gamma_{n}-\Gamma_{0})\mu_{n}\left\{dQ_{0}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})+dF_{0}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\right\}.

We then note that

\displaystyle\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}

\displaystyle\left\{dQ_{n}\,dF_{n}-dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}-dQ_{0}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})-dF_{0}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle=\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\left\{dQ_{n}\,dF_{n}-dQ_{0}\,dF_{n}-dF_{0}\,dQ_{n}+dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle=\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\,d(F_{n}-F_{0}),

which equals
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}
. Finally, we have

\displaystyle P_{0}(\Gamma_{n}\psi_{n})+\iint\Gamma_{n}\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}-P_{0}(\Gamma_{n}\theta_{0})

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left(\Gamma_{n}\frac{\mu_{0}-\mu_{n}}{g_{n}}\right)+\iint\Gamma_{n}(\mu_{n}-\mu_{0})\,dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}

\displaystyle=\iint\Gamma_{n}\left\{\frac{g_{0}}{g_{n}}(\mu_{0}-\mu_{n})+(\mu_{n}-\mu_{0})\right\}\,dQ_{0}\,dF_{0}

\displaystyle=\iint\Gamma_{n}\left(1-\frac{g_{0}}{g_{n}}\right)(\mu_{n}-\mu_{0})\,dQ_{0}\,dF_{0},

which equals
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}
. Putting these derivations back into equation (
9
), we have

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})

\displaystyle\left(\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}-\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}\right)+\left\{\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})+P_{0}\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}\right\}

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left\{(\Gamma_{n}-\Gamma_{0})\psi_{n}\right\}+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left\{\Gamma_{0}(\psi_{n}-\psi_{\infty})\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\iint\Gamma_{0}(\mu_{n}-\mu_{\infty})\left\{dQ_{0}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})+dF_{0}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\iint(\Gamma_{n}-\Gamma_{0})\mu_{n}\left\{dQ_{0}\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})+dF_{0}\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+R_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}+R_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}+R_{n,h,b,a_{0},6}

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left[\left\{\Gamma_{0}\left(\psi_{n}+\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}\right)+\int\Gamma_{0}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}-\left\{\Gamma_{0}\left(\psi_{\infty}+\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)+\int\Gamma_{0}\mu_{\infty}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left\{(\Gamma_{n}-\Gamma_{0})\left(\psi_{n}+\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}\right)+\int(\Gamma_{n}-\Gamma_{0})\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+R_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}+R_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}+R_{n,h,b,a_{0},6},

which equals
\sum_{j=2}^{6}R_{n,h,b,a_{0},j}
.
∎

Supplement E
Proof of Theorems

Proof of Theorem 1.

By Lemma
3
, we have

\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})=\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}+\sum_{j=1}^{6}R_{n,h,b,a_{0},j}.

By Lemma
7
,
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},1}=o\left(h^{2}\right)
. By Corollary
4
and Lemmas
17
,
19
, and
20
,
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},j}=o_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right)
for each
j\in\{2,\dotsc,6\}
. This establishes the first claim. By Lemma
5
,
(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}
converges in distribution to the claimed limit distribution. If
nh^{5}=O(1)
, then
(nh)^{1/2}R_{n,h,b,a_{0},1}=o\left(\{nh^{5}\}^{1/2}\right)=o(1)
, so

(nh)^{1/2}\left[\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})\right]=(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}+o_{p}(1),

so
(nh)^{1/2}\left[\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})\right]
converges to this same limit. For the final statement, by Lemma
21
,
\sigma_{n,h,b}^{2}(a_{0})=h\mathbb{P}_{n}(\phi_{n,h,b,a_{0}}^{*})^{2}
satisfies
\sigma_{n,h,b}^{2}(a_{0})-hP_{0}(\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*})^{2}=o_{p}(1)
. By Lemma
5
,
hP_{0}(\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*})^{2}
converges to the variance of the limit distribution. Hence,
\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})
converges in probability to the standard deviation of the limit distribution, so the final statement follows by Slutsky’s theorem.
∎

Proof of Theorem 2.

By Theorem 1 and since
nh^{5}=O(1)
, we have

(nh)^{1/2}\begin{pmatrix}\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{1})-\theta_{0}(a_{1})\\
\vdots\\
\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{m})-\theta_{0}(a_{m})\end{pmatrix}=(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{1}}^{*}\\
\vdots\\
\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{m}}^{*}\end{pmatrix}+o_{p}(1).

The result follows by Lemma
6
and Slutsky’s theorem.
∎

Proof of Theorem 3.

By Lemma
3
, we have

\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\theta_{n,h,b}(a)-\theta_{0}(a)\right|=\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a}^{*}+\sum_{j=1}^{6}R_{n,h,b,a,j}\right|.

By Lemma
7
,
\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|R_{n,h,b,a,1}\right|=O(h^{2+\delta_{4}})
for some
\delta_{4}>0
. By Corollary
6
and Lemmas
17
,
19
and
20
,
\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|R_{n,h,b,a,j}\right|=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
for
j\in\{2,\dots\,6\}
. We now write

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a}^{*}\right|

\displaystyle=(nh)^{-1/2}\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a)\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a}^{*}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq(nh)^{-1/2}\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a)\right|\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a}^{*}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a)}\right|

\displaystyle=(nh)^{-1/2}\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a)\right|\left[\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a}^{*}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a)}\right|-\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+(nh)^{-1/2}\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a)\right|\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a)\right|.

By Lemma
22
,
\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a)\right|=O(1)
, by Lemma
24
,
\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a)\right|=O_{p}(\{\log h^{-1}\}^{1/2})
, which is
O_{p}(\{\log n\}^{1/2})
since
nh\longrightarrow\infty
, and by Lemma
25
,
\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a}^{*}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a)}\right|-\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a)\right|=o_{p}(1)
. Hence,

\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a}^{*}\right|=O_{p}\left(\left\{nh/\log n\right\}^{-1/2}\right).

This proves the first claim. The second claim follows by Lemma
21
.

For the final claim, by the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}(nh)^{1/2}\left|\frac{\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})}{\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|\leq t\right)-P_{0}\left(\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{n,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\mid\mathbf{O}_{n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}(nh)^{1/2}\left|\frac{\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})}{\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|\leq t\right)-P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)-P_{0}\left(\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left(\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)-P_{0}\left(\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{n,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\mid\mathbf{O}_{n}\right)\right|.

The first term on the right hand side is
o(1)
by Lemma
27
. The second term on the right hand side is
o(1)
by Lemma
28
. The last term on the right hand side is
o_{p}(1)
by Lemma
29
.
∎

Supplement F
Analysis of the leading term

We first establish the following result on the behavior of
\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}
and
\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},3}
as
h,b\longrightarrow 0
.

Lemma 4
.

If conditions
(A1)
and
(A5)
hold, then
\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}=f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}+O(h)
and
c_{0,h,a_{0},2}=c_{2}+O(h)
as
h\longrightarrow 0
, and
\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}=f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{3}^{-1}+O(b)
as
b\longrightarrow 0
.

If conditions
(A1)
and
(A8)
hold, then
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}-f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}=O(h)
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|c_{0,h,a_{0},2}-c_{2}|=O(h)
, and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},2}-f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{3}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}=O(h)
as
h\longrightarrow 0
.

Proof of Lemma
4
.

The proof is analogous to that of Section 3.7 of
Fan and Gijbels, 1996
and many others. By definition of
\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}
and the change of variables
u=(t-a_{0})/h
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]

\displaystyle=\int\left(\frac{t-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}h^{-1}K\left(\frac{t-a_{0}}{h}\right)\,dF_{0}(t)=\int u^{j+k-2}K(u)f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du.

By
(A5)
,
f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)=f_{0}(a_{0})+O(uh)
, and since
K
has support
[-1,1]
by
(A1)
, we then have

\int u^{j+k-2}K(u)f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du=f_{0}(a_{0})\int u^{j+k-2}K(u)\,du+O(h)=f_{0}(a_{0})c_{j+k-2}+O(h).

Hence,
\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}=f_{0}(a_{0})\mathbf{S}_{2}+O(h)
, so
\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}=f_{0}(a)^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}+O(h)
since
f(a_{0})>0
. The proof for
\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}
is analogous. By the same logic, we have
P_{0}(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})=f_{0}(a_{0})(c_{2},c_{3})^{T}+O(h)
. Hence,

\displaystyle c_{0,h,a_{0},2}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}P_{0}(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})=e_{1}^{T}\left[f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}+O(h)\right]\left[f_{0}(a_{0})(c_{2},0)^{T}+O(h)\right]=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}(c_{2},0)^{T}+O(h)

\displaystyle=c_{2}+O(h).

For the uniform result, we have for each
j,k\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]-f_{0}(a_{0})c_{j+k+2}\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\int u^{j+k-2}K(u)\left[f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)-f_{0}(a_{0})\right]\,du\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\int\left|u\right|^{j+k-2}K(u)\left|f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)-f_{0}(a_{0})\right|\,du

\displaystyle\leq Ch

for some
C<\infty
because
f_{0}
is Lipschitz on
\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}
and
K
is uniformly bounded with compact support. The result follows, and a similar argument yields the results for
c_{0,h,a_{0},2}
and
\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}
.
∎

Lemma 5
.

If
(A1)
–
(A2)
,
(A4)
(a), and
(A5)
hold, then

(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}\indist\mathcal{N}\left(0,V_{K,\tau}f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})\right),

where

V_{K,\tau}:=\int\left\{K(u)-\tau^{3}c_{2}\frac{(\tau u)^{2}-c_{2}}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}K(\tau u)\right\}^{2}\,du=c_{0}^{*}-2\tau^{3}c_{2}\frac{\tau^{2}c_{2,\tau}^{*}-c_{2}c_{0,\tau}^{*}}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}+\tau^{5}c_{2}^{2}\frac{c_{4}^{*}-2c_{2}c_{2}^{*}+c_{2}^{2}c_{0}^{*}}{\left(c_{4}-c_{2}\right)^{2}}

is a positive, finite constant for all
\tau\in(0,\infty)
and kernels
K
satisfying
(A1)
. Furthermore,
hP_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}})^{2}
converges to
V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})/f_{0}(a_{0})
as
h\longrightarrow 0
.

Proof of Lemma
5
.

By adding and subtracting
\mathbb{P}_{n}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}
, we can rewrite
\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0})\right\}+\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right\}+\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\left(\mu_{\infty}-\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)\,dF_{0}\right\}

We show that only the first term contributes to the limit distribution; the remaining two terms are each
o_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})
. We use the Lyapunov central limit theorem for triangular arrays to demonstrate that the first term is asymptotically normal. The Lyapunov CLT implies that if for each
n
,
X_{n,1},X_{n,2},\dots X_{n,n}
are IID, mean-zero random variables that satisfy (1)
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{Var}(X_{n,i})=\Sigma>0
, and (2) there exists
\delta>0
such that
n^{-\delta/2}E|X_{n,i}|^{2+\delta}=o(1)
, then
n^{-1/2}\sum^{n}_{i=1}X_{n,i}
converges in distribution to
\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma)
. We can write
(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0})\right\}=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{n,i}
for

X_{n,i}:=h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A_{i})\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y_{i},A_{i},W_{i})-\theta_{0}(A_{i})\right\}.

Since
(A4)
(a) holds,
E_{0}\left[\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)\mid A=a\right]=\theta_{0}(a)
for
F_{0}
-almost every
a\in B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
by Lemma
2
, and by
(A1)
,
\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)=0
for all
a\notin B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
and
h\leq\delta_{1}
. Hence, for all
n
large enough,
E_{0}[X_{n,i}]=0
for all
i
. We thus have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(X_{n,i}\right)

\displaystyle=hE_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(A)^{2}\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)-\theta_{0}(A)\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=h\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a)\,dF_{0}(a)

\displaystyle=h\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)-e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\right\}^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a)f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle=\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}(1,u)^{T}K(u)-e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{3}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}(1,\{\tau_{n}u\},\{\tau_{n}u\}^{2})^{T}K(\tau_{n}u)\right\}^{2}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0}+uh)f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du.

By
(A5)
,
\sigma_{0}
and
f_{0}
are continuous at
a_{0}
. Also,
\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}
,
c_{0,h,a_{0},2}
, and
\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}
converge to
f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}
,
c_{2}
, and
f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{3}^{-1}
, respectively, as
b,h\longrightarrow 0
by Lemma
4
, and
\tau_{n}\longrightarrow\tau\in(0,\infty)
. The preceding display thus converges to

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}(1,u)^{T}K(u)-e_{3}^{T}c_{2}\tau^{3}\mathbf{S}_{3}^{-1}(1,\{\tau u\},\{\tau u\}^{2})^{T}K(\tau u)\right\}^{2}\,du.

Now,
e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}(1,u)^{T}=1
, and using the block structure of
\mathbf{S}_{3}
, we find that
e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{S}_{3}^{-1}=\frac{1}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}\left(-c_{2},0,1\right)
. Therefore, we can simplify the above to
\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}V_{K,\tau}
for

\displaystyle V_{K,\tau}

\displaystyle=\int\left\{K(u)-\tau^{3}c_{2}\frac{(\tau u)^{2}-c_{2}}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}K(\tau u)\right\}^{2}\,du

\displaystyle=\int K(u)^{2}\,du-2\tau^{3}c_{2}\frac{\int(\tau u)^{2}K(u)K(\tau u)\,du-c_{2}\int K(u)K(\tau u)\,du}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\tau^{6}c_{2}^{2}\frac{\int(\tau u)^{4}K(\tau u)^{2}\,du-2c_{2}\int(\tau u)^{2}K(\tau u)^{2}\,du+c_{2}^{2}\int K(\tau u)^{2}\,du}{\left(c_{4}-c_{2}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=c_{0}^{*}-2\tau^{3}c_{2}\frac{\tau^{2}c_{2,\tau}^{*}-c_{2}c_{0,\tau}^{*}}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}+\tau^{5}c_{2}^{2}\frac{c_{4}^{*}-2c_{2}c_{2}^{*}+c_{2}^{2}c_{0}^{*}}{\left(c_{4}-c_{2}\right)^{2}}.

Hence,
\mathrm{Var}(X_{n,i})
converges to
\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}V_{K,\tau}
as claimed. If
\tau=1
, then the above simplifies to

V_{K,1}=\int\left\{\frac{c_{4}-c_{2}u^{2}}{c_{4}-c_{2}^{2}}K(u)\right\}^{2}\,du=\frac{c_{0}^{*}c_{4}^{2}-2c_{2}c_{4}c_{2}^{*}+c_{2}^{2}c_{4}^{*}}{(c_{4}-c_{2}^{2})^{2}}.

Clearly,
V_{K,\tau}\geq 0
, with equality if and only if the expression in the integral is zero identically. When
\tau\neq 1
, the differing supports of
K(u)
and
K(\tau u)
guarantees that this is not the case, and if
\tau=1
then the expression is zero if and only if
c_{2}=c_{4}=0
, which is not the case. Hence,
V_{K,\tau}>0
for any
\tau\in(0,\infty)
. Furthermore, by the boundedness of
K
and since
c_{2}^{2}<c_{4}
by Jensen’s inequality,
V_{K,\tau}
is finite for every
\tau\in(0,\infty)
.

For the second condition of the Lyapunov CLT, we first note that

\displaystyle\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}E_{0}\left[\left|\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)-\theta_{0}(A)\right|^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a\right]\leq C.

for some
C<\infty
since
\mu_{\infty}
is uniformly bounded and
g_{\infty}
is uniformly bounded away from zero by
(A4)
(a) and
E_{0}(|Y|^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a,W=w)
is uniformly bounded over
a\in B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
and
w\in\mathcal{W}
by
(A5)
. Therefore, for all
n
large enough,

\displaystyle E|X_{n,i}|^{2+\delta_{2}}

\displaystyle=h^{1+\delta_{2}/2}\int\left|\Gamma_{0}(a)\right|^{2+\delta_{2}}E_{0}\left[\left|\xi_{\infty}(y,a,w)-\theta_{0}(a)\right|^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a\right]f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{1+\delta_{2}/2}\int\left|\Gamma_{0}(a)\right|^{2+\delta_{2}}f_{0}(a)\,da.

By the triangle inequality, we then have

\displaystyle\left\{\int\left|\Gamma_{0}(a)\right|^{2+\delta_{2}}f_{0}(a)\,da\right\}^{1/(2+\delta_{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\int\left|e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\right|^{2+\delta_{2}}f_{0}(a)\,da\right\}^{1/(2+\delta_{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left\{\int\left|e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\right|^{2+\delta_{2}}f_{0}(a)\,da\right\}^{1/(2+\delta_{2})}

\displaystyle=\left\{\int\left|e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}(1,u)^{T}K(u)\right|^{2+\delta_{2}}h^{-(1+\delta_{2})}f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du\right\}^{1/(2+\delta_{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left\{\int\left|e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}(1,u,u^{2})^{T}K(u)\right|^{2+\delta_{2}}h^{-(1+\delta_{2})}f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du\right\}^{1/(2+\delta_{2})}.

By Lemma
4
,
(A1)
,
(A2)
, and
(A5)
, the preceding display is bounded up to a constant by
h^{-(1+\delta_{2})/(2+\delta_{2})}
. Hence, for all
n
large enough,
E|X_{n,i}|^{2+\delta_{2}}
is bounded up to a constant by
h^{-\delta_{2}/2}
. Therefore,

n^{-\delta_{2}/2}E|X_{n,i}|^{2+\delta_{2}}=O\left(\left\{nh\right\}^{-\delta_{2}/2}\right)=o(1)

since
nh\longrightarrow\infty
by assumption.

Next, we show that
\mathbb{P}_{n}(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}})=O_{p}(\left\{n/h\right\}^{-1/2})
, which implies that it is
o_{p}(\left\{nh\right\}^{-1/2})
. We note that since
P_{0}(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0})=P_{0}\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
,
E_{0}\left[\mathbb{P}_{n}(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}})\right]=0
and
\mathrm{Var}\left[\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\right]=n^{-1}P_{0}\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)^{2}
. Thus, it is sufficient to show that
P_{0}(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}})^{2}=O(h)
.
We have

\displaystyle P_{0}(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}})^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq 2\int\left[e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0}}^{-1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\left\{\theta_{0}(a)-w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0}}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right\}\right]^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+2\int\left[e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\left\{\theta_{0}(a)-w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{b,a_{0},2}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right\}\right]^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+2\int\left[e_{1}^{T}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0}}^{-1}\left\{\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)-w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0}}^{-1}P_{0}(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})\right\}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\right]^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\times\left[e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=2h^{-1}\int\left[e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0}}^{-1}(1,u)^{T}K(u)\left\{\theta_{0}(a_{0}+uh)-(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0}}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right\}\right]^{2}f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+2b^{-1}\int\left[e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{T}K(u)\left\{\theta_{0}(a_{0}+ub)-\mathbf{u}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{b,a_{0},2}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right\}\right]^{2}f_{0}(a_{0}+ub)\,du

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+2h^{-1}\int\left[e_{1}^{T}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0}}^{-1}\left\{(u^{2},u^{3})^{T}-(1,u)(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0}}^{-1}P_{0}(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})\right\}K(u)\right]^{2}f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\times\left[e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0})\right]^{2}

where
\mathbf{u}_{2}
denotes the vector
(1,u,u^{2})
. For the first term, we define
\beta_{h,a_{0},1}:=(\theta_{0}(a_{0}),h\theta^{(1)}_{0}(a_{0}))^{T}
. We then write

\displaystyle(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0}}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)-\theta_{0}(a_{0})-uh\theta_{0}^{(1)}(a_{0})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)-(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\beta_{h,a_{0},1}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\left[P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)-P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}w_{h,a_{0},1}^{T}\right)\beta_{h,a_{0},1}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\left[\theta_{0}-w_{h,a_{0},1}^{T}\beta_{h,a_{0},1}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\left[\theta_{0}(a)-w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)^{T}\beta_{h,a_{0},1}\right]f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\int(1,v)^{T}K(v)\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0}+vh)-(1,v)^{T}\beta_{h,a_{0},1}\right]f_{0}(a_{0}+vh)\,dv

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\int(1,v)^{T}K(v)\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0}+vh)-\theta_{0}(a_{0})-vh\theta_{0}^{(1)}(a_{0})\right]f_{0}(a_{0}+vh)\,dv

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}o(h),

where we have also used
(A1)
and
(A5)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle h^{-1}\int\left[e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}(1,u)^{T}K(u)\left\{\theta_{0}(a_{0}+uh)-(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right\}\right]^{2}f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=h^{-1}\int\left[e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}(1,u)^{T}K(u)\left\{\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0}+uh)-\theta_{0}(a_{0})-uh\theta_{0}^{(1)}(a_{0})\right]\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left.\left.-\left[(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)-\theta_{0}(a_{0})-uh\theta_{0}^{(1)}(a_{0})\right]\right\}\right]^{2}f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=h^{-1}\int\left[e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}(1,u)^{T}K(u)o(h)\right]^{2}f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=o(h),

using Lemma
4
,
(A1)
,
(A2)
, and
(A5)
.

For the second term, we similarly define
\beta_{b,a_{0},2}:=(\theta_{0}(a_{0}),b\theta^{(1)}_{0}(a_{0}),0)^{T}
. By an identical calculation, we can then show that

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)-\theta_{0}(a_{0})-ub\theta_{0}^{(1)}(a_{0})=\mathbf{u}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}o(b)

and hence

\displaystyle b^{-1}\int\left[e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{T}K(u)\left\{\theta_{0}(a_{0}+ub)-\mathbf{u}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{b,a_{0},2}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right\}\right]^{2}f_{0}(a_{0}+ub)\,du=o(b).

Note that we can even show that this term is
o(b^{3})
using a second-order Taylor expansion, though this is unnecessary for the purposes of our proof.

A similar derivation as in the proof of Lemma
4
yields
P_{0}(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0})=\mathbf{S}_{2}e_{1}\theta_{0}(a_{0})f_{0}(a_{0})+O(b)
. Hence, using Lemma
4
, we have

\displaystyle\left[e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\left[\left\{e_{3}^{T}f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}+O(b)\right\}\left\{\mathbf{S}_{2}e_{1}\theta_{0}(a_{0})f_{0}(a_{0})+O(b)\right\}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\left[e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{2}e_{1}\theta_{0}(a_{0})+O(b)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=O(b^{2}).

In addition, it is straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\int\left[e_{1}^{T}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0}}^{-1}\left\{(u^{2},u^{3})^{T}-(1,u)(1,u)^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0}}^{-1}P_{0}(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})\right\}K(u)\right]^{2}f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du=O(1).

We now have
P_{0}(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}})^{2}=O(h)
.

Finally, we claim that

\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{\int\Gamma_{0}\left(\mu_{\infty}-\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)\,dF_{0}\right\}=O_{p}\left(n^{-1/2}\right),

which implies in particular that it is
o_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right)
. Since the function inside the empirical mean has mean zero, it is sufficient to show that

\int\left[\int\Gamma_{0}\left(\mu_{\infty}-\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)\,dF_{0}\right]^{2}\,dQ_{0}=O(1).

Since
\mu_{\infty}
is uniformly bounded by
(A4)
(a), we have

\displaystyle\int\left[\int\Gamma_{0}\left(\mu_{\infty}-\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)\,dF_{0}\right]^{2}\,dQ_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\int\left[\int\Gamma_{0}\mu_{\infty}\,dF_{0}\right]^{2}\,dQ_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\int\left[\int\left|e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0}}^{-1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\right|\,dF_{0}(a)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\left.+\int\left|e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\right|\,dF_{0}(a)\right]^{2}\,dQ_{0}(w)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\int\left|e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}(1,u)^{T}K(u)f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\right|\,du\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\left.+\int\left|e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}(1,u,u^{2})^{T}K(u)f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\right|\,du\right]^{2}.

This latter expression is
O(1)
by Lemma
4
,
(A1)
,
(A2)
, and
(A5)
.

For the final statement of the result, using the same decomposition of
\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}
as above and the fact that
E_{0}[\xi_{\infty}\mid A=a]=\theta_{0}(a)
for
a\in B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
, we can write

\displaystyle\sigma_{\infty,h,b}^{2}(a_{0})

\displaystyle=hP_{0}{\phi^{*2}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}}

\displaystyle=hP_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}^{2}\left(\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0}\right)^{2}\right]+hP_{0}\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)^{2}+hP_{0}\left[\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\mu_{\infty}-\smallint\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0})\,dF_{0}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+2hP_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\left(\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0}\right)\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\mu_{\infty}-\smallint\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0})\,dF_{0}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+2hP_{0}\left[\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\mu_{\infty}-\smallint\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0})\,dF_{0}\right].

We have showed that
hP_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}^{2}\left(\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0}\right)^{2}\right]
converges to
V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})/f_{0}(a_{0})
as
h\longrightarrow 0
. We have also showed that
hP_{0}\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)^{2}=O(h^{2})
and
hP_{0}\left[\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\mu_{\infty}-\smallint\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0})\,dF_{0}\right]^{2}=O(h)
, so the statement follows.

∎

Lemma 6
.

If the assumptions of Lemma
5
hold for each
a_{0}
in
\{a_{1},\dotsc,a_{m}\}
, then

(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{1}}^{*}\\
\vdots\\
\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{m}}^{*}\end{pmatrix}

converges in distribution to a mean-zero multivariate normal distribution with zero off-diagonal elements and diagonal elements
V_{K,\tau}f_{0}(a_{1})^{-1}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{1}),\dotsc,V_{K,\tau}f_{0}(a_{m})^{-1}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{m})
. Furthermore,
hP_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{j}}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{k}})
converges to zero for
j\neq k
.

Proof of Lemma
6
.

We focus on the case of
m=2
for simplicity; the proof for
m>2
is entirely analogous. We will show that for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,

t_{1}(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{1}}+t_{2}(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{2}}\indist t_{1}^{2}V_{K,\tau}f_{0}(a_{1})^{-1}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{1})Z_{1}+t_{2}^{2}V_{K,\tau}f_{0}(a_{2})^{-1}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{2})Z_{2}

where
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
are independent standard normal random variables. By the Cramer-Wold device, this yields the result. First, by the derivations in the proof of Lemma
5
,

\displaystyle t_{1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{1}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{t_{1}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{1}}(\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0})\right\}+o_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})\hskip 18.49988pt\text{and}

\displaystyle t_{2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{t_{2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{2}}(\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0})\right\}+o_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2}).

Hence,

t_{1}(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{1}}+t_{2}(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{2}}=(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{(t_{1}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{1}}+t_{2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{2}})(\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0})\right\}+o_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2}).

As in the proof of Lemma
5
, we demonstrate the convergence of the expression on the right using the Lyapunov CLT for triangular arrays. We define
X_{n,1},X_{n,2},\dotsc,X_{n,n}
as

X_{n,i}:=h^{1/2}\left\{t_{1}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{1}}(A_{i})+t_{2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{2}}(A_{i})\right\}\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y_{i},A_{i},W_{i})-\theta_{0}(A_{i})\right\}.

We can then write
(nh)^{1/2}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{(t_{1}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{1}}+t_{2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{2}})(\xi_{\infty}-\theta_{0})\right\}
as
n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{X}_{n,i}
. Hence, the claim follows by showing that
n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{n,i}
converges in distribution to the claimed limit, which we do using the Lyapunov CLT.

The main condition of the Lyapunov CLT that differs from Lemma
5
is convergence of the variance. By linearity of expectation and since
P_{0}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{1}}=P_{0}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{2}}=0
, we have
E_{0}[X_{n,i}]=0
for all
i
. We also have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(X_{n,i}\right)

\displaystyle=hE_{0}\left[\left\{t_{1}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{1}}(A)+t_{2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{2}}(A)\right\}^{2}\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)-\theta_{0}(A)\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=ht_{1}^{2}E_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{1}}(A)^{2}\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)-\theta_{0}(A)\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+ht_{2}^{2}E_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{2}}(A)^{2}\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)-\theta_{0}(A)\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+2ht_{1}t_{2}E_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{1}}(A)\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{2}}(A)\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)-\theta_{0}(A)\right\}^{2}\right].

The first two terms converge to
t_{1}^{2}V_{K,\tau}f_{0}(a_{1})^{-1}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{1})
and
t_{2}^{2}V_{K,\tau}f_{0}(a_{2})^{-1}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{2})
by Lemma
5
. We can write out the third term as

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{1}}(A)\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{2}}(A)\left\{\xi_{\infty}(Y,A,W)-\theta_{0}(A)\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{1},1}^{-1}w_{h,a_{1},1}(a)K_{h,a_{1}}(a)e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{2},1}^{-1}w_{h,a_{2},1}(a)K_{h,a_{2}}(a)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left.-e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{1},3}^{-1}w_{b,a_{1},3}(a)K_{b,a_{1}}(a)e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{2},1}^{-1}w_{h,a_{2},1}(a)K_{h,a_{2}}(a)\right.
