\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left.-e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{1},1}^{-1}w_{h,a_{1},1}(a)K_{h,a_{1}}(a)e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{2},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{2},2}(a)K_{b,a_{2}}(a)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left.+e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{1},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{1},2}(a)K_{b,a_{1}}(a)e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{2},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{2},2}(a)K_{b,a_{2}}(a)\right\}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a)f_{0}(a)\,da.

The four summands in the above display involve the products
K_{h,a_{1}}K_{h,a_{2}}
,
K_{h,a_{1}}K_{b,a_{2}}
,
K_{b,a_{1}}K_{h,a_{2}}
, and
K_{b,a_{1}}K_{b,a_{2}}
. Since the support of
K
is contained in
[-1,1]
, each of these products is zero for all
h,b
small enough. Specifically, if
\max\{h,b\}<|a_{1}-a_{2}|/2
, then
\{a:K_{h,a_{1}}(a)>0\}\cap\{a:K_{h,a_{2}}(a)>0\}=\{a:|a-a_{1}|\leq h\}\cap\{a:|a-a_{2}|\leq h\}=\emptyset
. Therefore, the variance converges to
t_{1}^{2}V_{K,\tau}f_{0}(a_{1})^{-1}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{1})+t_{2}^{2}V_{K,\tau}f_{0}(a_{2})^{-1}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{2})
. Each term is bounded away from zero by Lemma
5
. The remainder of the conditions of the Lyapunov CLT can be checked using the same derivations as in Lemma
5
and using the triangle inequality.
∎

Supplement G
Analysis of remainder term
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},1}

Lemma 7
.

If
(A1)
and
(A5)
hold, then
\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})=o(h^{2})
. If
(A1)
and
(A8)
hold, then for some
\delta_{4}>0
,
sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})\right|=O(h^{2+\delta_{4}})
.

Proof of Lemma
7
.

By
(A5)
, the second derivative of
a\mapsto\theta_{0}(a)
is continuous in a neighborhood of
a=a_{0}
, so by the mean value form of Taylor’s theorem, for each
a
in a neighborhood of
a_{0}
, there exists
a_{*}
(depending on
a
) between
a_{0}
and
a
such that

\theta_{0}(a)-\theta_{0}(a_{0})=\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{*})(a-a_{0})^{2}.

We then have

\displaystyle\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})

\displaystyle=\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\theta_{0}(a)f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle=\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0})+\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})^{2}\right]f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\tfrac{1}{2}\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\left[\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{*})-\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\right](a-a_{0})^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da.

We show that the first term equals
\theta_{0}(a_{0})
. We have

\displaystyle\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0})+\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})^{2}\right]f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0})+\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})^{2}\right]f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}(h/b)^{2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\int w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0})+\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})^{2}\right]f_{0}(a)\,da.

By the definitions of
w_{h,a_{0},1}
,
\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}
, and
c_{0,h,a_{0},2}
, we then have

\displaystyle e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0})+\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})^{2}\right]f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)^{T}f_{0}(a)\,da\,\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0}),h\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\tfrac{1}{2}h^{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\int w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\left[(a-a_{0})/h\right]^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\,\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0}),\,h\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})\right]+\tfrac{1}{2}h^{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})c_{0,h,a_{0},2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\theta_{0}(a_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}h^{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})c_{0,h,a_{0},2}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\int w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0})+\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})^{2}\right]f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\int w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)^{T}f_{0}(a)\,da\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0}),\,\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0}),\,\tfrac{1}{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0}),\,b\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0}),\,\tfrac{1}{2}b^{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\tfrac{1}{2}b^{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\left[\theta_{0}(a_{0})+\theta_{0}^{\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})(a-a_{0})^{2}\right]f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\theta_{0}(a_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2}h^{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})c_{0,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}(h/b)^{2}\tfrac{1}{2}b^{2}\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\theta_{0}(a_{0}).

Hence,

\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})=\tfrac{1}{2}h^{2}\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\left[\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{*})-\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\right][(a-a_{0})/h]^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da.

This is
o(h^{2})
by continuity of
\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}
at
a_{0}
.

For the uniform statement, using the above derivations, since
K
is uniformly bounded, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\theta_{0,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\lesssim h^{2}\left[\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}+c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\right]\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\sup_{|a_{1}-a_{0}|\leq h}\left|\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{1})-\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}(a_{0})\right|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|f_{0}(a_{0})|.

By Lemma
4
and
(A8)
, this is
o(h^{2+\delta_{4}})
for
\delta_{4}
the Hölder exponent of
\theta_{0}^{\prime\prime}
.

∎

Supplement H
Analysis of remainder terms
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}
and
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},3}

Before presenting results regarding the remainder terms
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}
and
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},3}
, we state a Lemma regarding the rate of convergence of
\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}
.

Lemma 8
.

Denote by
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
the element-wise maximum norm. If
(A1)
and
(A5)
hold and
nh\longrightarrow\infty
, then
\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}
and
\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}
are
O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})
,
\|\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\|_{\infty}
and
\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\|_{\infty}
are
O_{p}(\{nb\}^{-1/2})
, and
c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}=O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})
.

Proof of Lemma
8
.

We first note that for all
(j,k)
,
E_{0}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]\right)=\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]
.
Using the change of variables
u=(a-a_{0})/h
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\left\{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{A_{i}-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}h^{-1}K\left(\frac{A_{i}-a_{0}}{h}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=(nh)^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Var}\left\{\left(\frac{A_{i}-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}K\left(\frac{A_{i}-a_{0}}{h}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq(nh)^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{0}\left[\left\{\left(\frac{A_{i}-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}K\left(\frac{A_{i}-a_{0}}{h}\right)\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=n^{-1}h^{-2}\int\left\{\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)\right\}^{2}\,dF_{0}(a)

\displaystyle=(nh)^{-1}\int u^{2(j+k-2)}K^{2}\left(u\right)f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du.

Since
f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)=f_{0}(a_{0})+O(uh)
by
(A5)
and
K
is uniformly bounded with compact support by
(A1)
, we have

\displaystyle(nh)^{-1}\int u^{2(j+k-2)}K^{2}\left(u\right)f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle=(nh)^{-1}f_{0}(a_{0})\left\{\int u^{2(j+k-2)}K^{2}\left(u\right)\,du+O(h)\right\}

\displaystyle=(nh)^{-1}f_{0}(a_{0})c_{2(i+j-2),2}+o(\{nh\}^{-1})

for
1\leq j,k,\leq 2
. By Chebyshev’s inequality, we then have
\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]=O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})
. Since this holds for all
1\leq j,k,\leq 2
, we conclude that
\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})
. The result for
\|\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\{nb\}^{-1/2})
can be shown analogously.

To show that
\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1})
, we first write
\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}=\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}(\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1})\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}
. Denoting by
\|\cdot\|_{1}
the
L_{1}
matrix operator norm, it follows from the fact that the
L_{1}
norm is sub-multiplicative,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}(\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1})\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}(\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1})\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\|_{1}\|\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\|_{1}\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{1}.

We then note that
f_{0}(a_{0})>0
implies that
\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{1}=O(1)
by Lemma
4
. Since
nh\longrightarrow\infty
, Lemma
4
also implies that
\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\inprob f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}
, so by the continuous mapping theorem,
\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\|_{1}\inprob\|f_{0}(a_{0})^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}\|_{1}
and hence
\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\|_{1}=O_{p}(1)
. We conclude that
\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})
. The derivation above holds for finite dimensional matrices, and thus a similar argument yields that
\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\{nb\}^{-1/2})
.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right)\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)+e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right).

Both terms are
O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})
by the calculations above.
∎

We establish rates of convergence of
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}
and
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},3}
using empirical process theory. Before presenting supporting lemmas, we review key definitions and notation used in empirical process theory. For a class of functions
\mathcal{H}
defined on a domain
\mathcal{X}
, a probability measure
Q
on
\mathcal{X}
, and any
\varepsilon>0
, the
\varepsilon
-covering number
N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{H},L_{2}(Q))
of
\mathcal{H}
relative to the
L_{2}(Q)
metric is the smallest number of
L_{2}(Q)
-balls of radius less than or equal to
\varepsilon
needed to cover
\mathcal{H}
. A function
H
on
\mathcal{X}
is called an
envelope function
for
\mathcal{H}
if
\sup_{\eta\in\mathcal{H}}|\eta(x)|\leq H(x)
for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
.

We first consider the sequence of stochastic processes
\{V_{n}(\lambda):\lambda\in\Lambda\}
, for
V_{n}(\lambda):=\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}
, where

\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}(y,a,w):=h^{1/2}\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\lambda(y,a,w).

We note that
\eta
depends on
n
through
h
. We also make use of the following semi-metric on
\Lambda
:

\rho_{a_{0},\varepsilon}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}):=\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\varepsilon}\left(E_{0}\left[\left\{\lambda_{1}(Y,A,W)-\lambda_{2}(Y,A,W)\right\}^{2}\mid A=a\right]\right)^{1/2}.

We then have the following lemma.

Lemma 9
.

Suppose
(A1)
,
(A2)
, and
(A5)
hold, and that
\Lambda
is a class of functions with envelope
L
satisfying:

1.

\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}E_{0}[L^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a]<\infty
for some
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}>0
; and

2.

\int_{0}^{1}\left\{\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon\|L\|_{Q,2},\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon<\infty
.

Then for each
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
,
\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}|=O_{p}(1)
, and for any possibly random sequences
\lambda_{n1},\lambda_{n2}\in\Lambda
such that
\rho_{a_{0},\varepsilon}(\lambda_{n1},\lambda_{n2})=o_{p}(1)
for some
\varepsilon>0
,
\mathbb{G}_{n}\{\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n1}}-\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n2}}\}=o_{p}(1)
, so
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\lambda_{1n}-\lambda_{2n})\right\}=o_{p}(1)
as well.

Proof of Lemma
9
.

We first establish properties of the class
\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j}:=\{\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}:\lambda\in\Lambda\}
. Since
L
is an envelope for
\Lambda
,

H_{h,a_{0},j}(y,a,w):=h^{1/2}\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)L(y,a,w)

is an envelope for
\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j}
. By the tower property and a change of variables, we then have for all
h<\delta_{1}

\displaystyle P_{0}H_{h,a_{0},j}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int h\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{2(j-1)}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)^{2}E_{0}\left[L^{2}\mid A=a\right]\,f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle=\int u^{2(j-1)}K(u)^{2}E_{0}\left[L^{2}\mid A=a_{0}+uh\right]\,f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}E_{0}\left[L^{2}\mid A=a\right]\int u^{2(j-1)}K(u)^{2}\,f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du.

The last expression above is
O(1)
by assumption,
(A1)
, and
(A5)
.

Next, we study the uniform entropy of the class
\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j}
. We clearly have
\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j}=\mathcal{G}_{h,a_{0},j}\Lambda
, where
\mathcal{G}_{h,a_{0},j}
is the class consisting of the single function
a\mapsto h^{1/2}[(a-a_{0})/h]^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)
. We then have by, e.g. Lemma 5.1 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
, that

\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|H_{h,a_{0},j}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j},L_{2}(Q)\right)\leq\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|L\|_{Q,2},\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)

for any
\varepsilon>0
. We can now establish the first claim of Lemma
9
. By Theorem 2.14.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, we have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},h,\lambda}\right|\right]

\displaystyle=E_{0}\left[\sup_{\eta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\{P_{0}H_{h,a_{0},j}^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\sup_{Q}\int_{0}^{1}\left\{1+\log N\left(\varepsilon\|H_{h,a_{0},j}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j},L_{2}(Q)\right)\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq O(1)\int_{0}^{1}\left\{\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon\|L\|_{Q,2},\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon.

The integral is finite by assumption, which establishes the claim.

We will establish the second statement by showing that the sequence of processes is
asymptotically uniformly
\rho_{a_{0},\varepsilon}
-equicontinuous

(
Pollard, 1984
;
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
)
. To do so, we will use Theorem 2.11.1 (see also Theorem 2.11.22) of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, which implies that
\{V_{n}:n=1,2,\dots\}
is asymptotically uniformly
\rho_{a_{0},\varepsilon}
-equicontinuous if the following conditions hold:

(a)

P_{0}H_{h,a_{0},j}^{2}=O(1)
;

(b)

P_{0}\left[H_{h,a_{0},j}^{2}I\{H_{h,a_{0},j}>\varepsilon n^{1/2}\}\right]=o(1)
for every
\varepsilon>0
;

(c)

\sup_{\rho_{a_{0},\varepsilon}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})<\delta_{n}}P_{0}\{\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{1}}-\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{2}}\}^{2}=o(1)
for every
\delta_{n}\longrightarrow 0
; and

(d)

for every
\delta_{n}=o(1)
,
\sup_{Q}\int_{0}^{\delta_{n}}\left\{\log N(\varepsilon\|H_{h,a_{0},j}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j},L_{2}(Q))\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon=o(1)
.

We prove that
\{\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}:\lambda\in\Lambda\}
is
\rho_{a_{0},\varepsilon}
-equicontinuous by establishing these conditions, and the result follows.

We showed condition (a) above. For (b), we have again by the tower property and a change of variables

\displaystyle P_{0}\left\{H_{h,a_{0},j}^{2}I\left(H_{h,a_{0},j}>\varepsilon n^{1/2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\int h\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{2(j-1)}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)^{2}E_{0}\left[L^{2}I\left(H_{h,a_{0},j}>\varepsilon n^{1/2}\right)\mid A=a\right]\,f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle=\int u^{2(j-1)}K(u)^{2}E_{0}\left[L^{2}I\left(H_{h,a_{0},j}>\varepsilon n^{1/2}\right)\mid A=a_{0}+uh\right]\,f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du.

Now we note that since the kernel
K
is uniformly bounded by, say
\bar{K}<\infty
, and has support contained in
[-1,1]
,

I\left(H_{h,a_{0},j}>\varepsilon n^{1/2}\right)=I\left(h^{1/2}[(A-a_{0})/h]^{j-1}K_{h,a_{0}}(A)L>\varepsilon n^{1/2}\right)\leq I\left(\bar{K}L>\varepsilon(nh)^{1/2}\right).

Hence, for all
h<\delta_{1}
,

\displaystyle P_{0}\left\{H_{h,a_{0},j}^{2}I\left(H_{h,a_{0},j}>\varepsilon n^{1/2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{K}^{2}\int_{-1}^{1}E_{0}\left[L^{2}I\left(L>\varepsilon(nh)^{1/2}/\bar{K}\right)\mid A=a_{0}+uh\right]\,f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}E_{0}\left[L^{2}I\left(L>\varepsilon(nh)^{1/2}/\bar{K}\right)\mid A=a\right].

Then by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[L^{2}I\left(L>\varepsilon(nh)^{1/2}/\bar{K}\right)\mid A=a\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{E_{0}\left[L^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a\right]\right\}^{2/(2+\delta_{2})}\left\{P_{0}\left(L>\varepsilon(nh)^{1/2}/\bar{K}\mid A=a\right)\right\}^{\delta_{2}/(2+\delta_{2})},

and by Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle P_{0}\left(L>\varepsilon(nh)^{1/2}/\bar{K}\mid A=a\right)

\displaystyle\leq E_{0}\left[L^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a\right]/\left[\varepsilon(nh)^{1/2}/\bar{K}\right]^{2+\delta_{2}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}E_{0}\left[L^{2}I\left(L>\varepsilon(nh)^{1/2}/\bar{K}\right)\mid A=a\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left[\varepsilon(nh)^{1/2}/\bar{K}\right]^{-\delta_{2}}\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}E_{0}\left[L^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a\right].

Since
nh\longrightarrow\infty
, this latter expression tends to zero by assumption for every
\varepsilon>0
, which verifies (b).

For (c), by a similar calculation we have for all
h
small enough that

\displaystyle P_{0}\left\{\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{1}}-\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{2}}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int h\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{2(j-1)}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)^{2}E_{0}\left[\left\{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}\right\}^{2}\mid A=a\right]f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle=\int u^{2(j-1)}K(u)^{2}E_{0}\left[\left\{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}\right\}^{2}\mid A=a_{0}+uh\right]f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\lesssim C\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\varepsilon}E_{0}\left[\left\{\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}\right\}^{2}\mid A=a\right]

\displaystyle=\rho_{a_{0},\varepsilon}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})^{2}.

Hence,

\sup_{\rho(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})<\delta_{n}}P_{0}\{\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{1}}-\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{2}}\}^{2}\lesssim\delta_{n}^{2},

so that (c) is satisfied.

For (d), using the entropy bound established above, we have

\sup_{Q}\int_{0}^{\delta}\left\{\log N\left(\varepsilon\|H_{h,a_{0},j}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j},L_{2}(Q)\right)\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon\leq\int_{0}^{\delta}\left\{\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon\|L\|_{Q,2},\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon.

Since the integral on the right is convergent by assumption, the expression converges to zero as
\delta\to 0
, which verifies (d).

For the final statement, by Lemma
4
, we can write

\displaystyle h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\lambda

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{2}C_{h,a_{0},j}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}C_{h,b,a_{0},j}^{\prime}\eta_{b,a_{0},j,\lambda}

for some constants
C_{h,a_{0},j}=O(1)
and
C_{h,b,a_{0},j}^{\prime}=O(1)
. By the result above, we then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\lambda_{1n}-\lambda_{2n})\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{2}C_{h,a_{0},j}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n1}}-\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n2}}\right\}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}C_{h,b,a_{0},j}^{\prime}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\eta_{b,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n1}}-\eta_{b,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n2}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{2}O(1)o_{p}(1)+\sum_{j=1}^{3}O(1)o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1).

∎

Next, we consider the sequence of stochastic processes
\{\bar{V}_{n}(\lambda):\lambda\in\Lambda\}
, for
\bar{V}_{n}(\lambda):=\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\eta}_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}
, where

\bar{\eta}_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}(y,a,w):=\int h^{1/2}\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\lambda(y,a,w)\,dF_{0}(a).

Lemma 10
.

Suppose
(A1)
,
(A2)
, and
(A5)
hold, and that
\Lambda
is a class of functions uniformly bounded by
L<\infty
and satisfying
\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon,\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{-V}
for some
C<\infty
and
V\in(0,2)
. Then
\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\eta}_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}\right|=o_{p}(h^{(2-s)/(2s)})
for every
s\in(V\vee 1,2)
and each
j\in\{1,2,\ldots\}
. Hence,
\sup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\int h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\lambda\,dF_{0}\right)\right|=o_{p}(h^{(2-s)/(2s)})
for every
s\in(V,2)
as well.

Proof of Lemma
10
.

We let
\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,a_{0},j}:=\{\bar{\eta}_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}:\lambda\in\Lambda\}
. We use Theorem 2.14.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
. We first bound the uniform entropy of
\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,a_{0},j}
. Lemma 5.1 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
implies that the class of functions
\mathcal{F}_{h,a_{0},j}:=\left\{(y,a,w)\mapsto h^{1/2}\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\lambda(y,a,w):\lambda\in\Lambda\right\}
satisfies

\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|F_{h,a_{0},j}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F}_{h,a_{0},j},L_{2}(Q)\right)\leq\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon L,\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)

relative to the envelope function
F_{h,a_{0},j}:a\mapsto Lh^{1/2}|(a-a_{0})/h|^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)
. We note that for
s\geq 1
,

\left[\int F_{h,a_{0},j}(a)^{s}\,dF_{0}(a)\right]^{1/s}=Lh^{1/2+(1-s)/s}\left[\int|u|^{s(j-1)}K(u)^{s}f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du\right]^{1/s}\lesssim h^{(2-s)/(2s)}

by
(A1)
and
(A5)
. Hence,
\bar{H}_{h,s}:=Ch^{(2-s)/(2s)}
is an envelope for
\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,a_{0},j}
for some
C<\infty
. By Lemma 5.2 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
(with
r=t=2
), the above bound for the uniform entropy of
\mathcal{F}_{h,a_{0},j}
, and the assumed bound for the uniform entropy of
\Lambda
, we then have

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon\|\bar{H}_{h,s}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j},L_{2}(Q)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon^{2/s}\|F_{h,a_{0},j}\|_{Q,2}/2^{r/s},\mathcal{F}_{h,a_{0},j},L_{2}(Q)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon^{2/s}L/2^{r/s},\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2V/s}.

Theorem 2.14.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
then gives

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{\bar{\eta}\in\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,a_{0},j}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\eta}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bar{H}_{h,s}\|_{P,2}\int_{0}^{1}\left\{1+\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon\|\bar{H}_{h,s}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{H}_{h,a_{0},j},L_{2}(Q)\right)\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{(2-s)/(2s)}\int_{0}^{1}\left\{1+\varepsilon^{-2V/s}\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon.

The integral is finite so long as
V/s<1
. Thus,

E_{0}\left[\sup_{\bar{\eta}\in\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,a_{0},j}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\eta}\right|\right]=O\left(h^{(2-s)/(2s)}\right)

for all
s>V
. Furthermore, for any
s>V
, there exists
s^{\prime}\in(V,s)
, so that

E_{0}\left[\sup_{\bar{\eta}\in\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,a_{0},j}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\eta}\right|\right]=O\left(h^{(2-s^{\prime})/(2s^{\prime})}\right)=o\left(h^{(2-s)/(2s)}\right).

The final statement regarding
\{\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(\int h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\lambda\,dF_{0}\right):\lambda\in\Lambda\}
follows by decomposing
\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}
and using Lemma
8
, as in the proof of Lemma
9
.
∎

We now use these results to establish rates of convergence of the remainder terms
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}
and
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},3}
.

Corollary 4
.

If
(A1)
–
(A5)
hold, then
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}=o_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right)
and
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},3}=O_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1}\right)
.

Proof of Corollary
4
.

We can write

\displaystyle(nh)^{1/2}R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\left(\psi_{n}-\psi_{\infty}\right)\right\}+\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\left(\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}-\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}-\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu_{\infty}\,dF_{0}\right\}

(10)

and

\displaystyle(nh)^{1/2}R_{n,h,b,a_{0},3}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\psi_{n}\right\}+\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}

(11)

We address the first two terms in each expansion using Lemma
9
. For the first term, we set
\Lambda:=\{o\mapsto[y-\mu(a,w)]/g(a,w):\mu\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu},g\in\mathcal{F}_{g}\}
. By
(A3)
, an envelope function for
\Lambda
is
L(y,a,w)=C+C^{\prime}|y|
for some
C,C^{\prime}<\infty
. Hence, by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}E_{0}\left[L^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}E_{0}\left[\left\{C+C^{\prime}|Y|\right\}^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left[C+C^{\prime}\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left(E_{0}\left\{|Y|^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a\right\}\right)^{1/(2+\delta_{2})}\right]^{2+\delta_{2}},

which is finite by
(A5)
. Therefore, condition 1 of Lemma
9
holds. For condition 2 of Lemma
9
, we note that permanence properties of uniform entropy numbers and uniform entropy integrals (see, e.g., Theorem 2.10.20 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
and Lemma 5.1 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
) in conjunction with
(A3)
implies that
\Lambda
possesses finite uniform entropy integral. Finally, we show that
\rho_{a_{0},\delta_{1}}(\psi_{n},\psi_{\infty})=o_{p}(1)
. By Jensen’s inequality,

E_{0}\left(Y^{2}\mid A=a,W=w\right)\leq\left[E_{0}\left(|Y|^{2+\delta_{2}}\mid A=a,W=w\right)\right]^{2/(2+\delta_{2})}

which is uniformly bounded for
a\in B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
and
P_{0}
-almost every
w
by
(A5)
. We also note that by
(A3)
and
(A4)
, for any functions
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
of
(a,w)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[\left\{\gamma_{1}(a,W)-\gamma_{2}(a,W)\right\}^{2}\mid A=a\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[g_{0}(a,W)\left\{\gamma_{1}(a,W)-\gamma_{2}(a,W)\right\}^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[\left\{\gamma_{1}(a,W)-\gamma_{2}(a,W)\right\}^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[I_{B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})\times\mathcal{W}}(a,W)\left\{\gamma_{1}(a,W)-\gamma_{2}(a,W)\right\}^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[I_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}(a,W)\left\{\gamma_{1}(a,W)-\gamma_{2}(a,W)\right\}^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{3}d\left(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{j}\right).

We then have

\displaystyle\rho_{a_{0},\delta_{1}}(\psi_{n},\psi_{\infty})

\displaystyle=\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[\left(\psi_{n}-\psi_{0}\right)^{2}\mid A=a\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[\left(\{1/(g_{n}g_{\infty})\}\left\{Y-\mu_{n}\right\}\left\{g_{\infty}-g_{n}\right\}-\{1/g_{\infty}\}\{\mu_{n}-\mu_{\infty}\}\right)^{2}\mid A=a\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[Y^{2}\left(g_{n}-g_{\infty}\right)^{2}\mid A=a\right]\right\}^{1/2}+\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[\mu_{n}^{2}\left(g_{n}-g_{\infty}\right)^{2}\mid A=a\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[\left(\mu_{n}-\mu_{\infty}\right)^{2}\mid A=a\right]\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{E_{0}\left[E_{0}\left(Y^{2}\mid A=a,W\right)\left(g_{n}-g_{\infty}\right)^{2}\mid A=a\right]\right\}^{1/2}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}d\left(g_{n},g_{\infty};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sum_{j=1}^{3}d\left(\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{3}d\left(g_{n},g_{\infty};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{j}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{3}d\left(\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{j}\right).

Hence, by
(A4)
,
\rho_{a_{0},\delta_{1}}(\psi_{n},\psi_{\infty})=o_{p}(1)
. Lemma
9
thus implies that
\mathbb{G}_{n}\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\psi_{n}-\psi_{\infty})\}=o_{p}(1)
, which addresses the first term in equation (
10
). For the first term in equation (
11
), we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\psi_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right)\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}(h/b)^{2}e_{3}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},2}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right)\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\left(c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\right)(h/b)^{2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}.

Since we have established that the conditions of Lemma
9
hold for the class
\Lambda
that
\psi_{n}
falls in, Lemma
9
implies that all elements of
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}
and
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}
are
O_{p}(1)
. In conjunction with Lemma
8
, we then have

\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\psi_{n}\right\}=O_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right).

For the second term in equation (
10
), we use Lemma
9
with
\Lambda:=\left\{a\mapsto\int\mu(a,w)\,dQ_{0}(w):\mu\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}\right\}
, which is a uniformly bounded class with envelope
L=C_{1}
by
(A3)
. Hence, condition (1) of Lemma
9
hold trivially for this class. For condition (2),
(A3)
and Lemma 5.2 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
(with
r=s=t=2
) together imply that condition (2) of Lemma
9
is satisfied. Therefore, if
\rho_{a_{0},\delta_{1}}(\lambda_{n},\lambda_{\infty})=o_{p}(1)
for
\lambda_{n}:a\mapsto\int\mu_{n}(a,w)\,dQ_{0}(w)
and
\lambda_{\infty}:a\mapsto\int\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\,dQ_{0}(w)
, Lemma
9
implies that

\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\left(\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}-\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)\right\}=o_{p}(1).

Since
\lambda_{n}
and
\lambda_{\infty}
are functions just of
a
, we have

\displaystyle\rho_{a_{0},\delta_{1}}(\lambda_{n},\lambda_{\infty})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\left\{\int\left[\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\right]\,dQ_{0}(w)\right\}^{2}\leq\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}\int\left[\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{\infty}(a,w)\right]^{2}\,dQ_{0}(w),

which is
o_{p}(1)
by
(A4)
as noted above. A similar argument yields that the second term of (
11
) is
O_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right)
.

For the third and fourth terms in equation (
10
), we use Lemma
10
with
\Lambda=\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
. The conditions of the lemma are satisfied by
(A3)
, so that

\sup_{\mu\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|=o_{p}\left(h^{(2-s)/(2s)}\right)

for every
s\in(V_{\mu},2)
. Since
\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
almost surely for all
n
large enough, the result follows.

For the third term in equation (
11
), we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right)\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}(h/b)^{2}e_{3}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right)\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}w_{h,a_{0},2}K_{h,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-(c_{0,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2})(h/b)^{2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}w_{h,a_{0},2}K_{h,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}

Each of the terms in
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}
and
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},3}K_{b,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}
are
o_{p}\left(h^{(2-s)/(2s)}\right)
by Lemma
10
, so they are also
O_{p}(1)
. In conjunction with Lemma
8
, we then have

\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}=O_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right),

as claimed.
∎

We now turn to uniform control of
R_{n,h,b,s,2}
and
R_{n,h,b,,3}
over
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a class of measurable functions equipped with a measurable envelope
F
. We say that
\mathcal{F}
is
VC type
with envelope
F
if there exists a constant
V>0
such that
\sup_{Q}N(\varepsilon\|F\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q))\lesssim\varepsilon^{-V}
when
\varepsilon<1
and the supremum is taken over all probability measures.

Lemma 11
.

If
(A1)
holds, then
\{a\mapsto(\tfrac{a-a_{0}}{h})^{j-1}K(\tfrac{a-a_{0}}{h}):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
is VC type for each fixed
h>0
and positive integer
j
.

Proof of Lemma
11
.

By
(A1)
,
K
is supported on
[-1,1]
and is uniformly bounded. Hence,

\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)=I\left(\left|\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right|\leq 1\right)\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right),

which implies that the class in question is contained in the product of the two classes of functions

\displaystyle\left\{K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\right\}\text{ and }\left\{\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}I\left(\left|\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right|\leq 1\right):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\right\}.

The first class is VC type by
(A1)
and the results of
Giné and Guillou, 2002
. For the second class, we first rewrite this class as

\left\{\phi\circ p_{j}\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\right\},

where where
\phi:=u\mapsto uI(|u|\leq 1)
and
p_{j}:=u\mapsto u^{j}
. Since
\phi
is a bounded real function of bounded variation and
p_{j}
is a polynomial,
Giné and Guillou, 2002
implies that this class is also VC. Since the product of VC classes is also VC, (e.g., Lemma 5.1 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
), the result follows.
∎

Corollary 5
.

For
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
and
h,b>0
, consider the following function:

\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}:(y,a,w)\mapsto h^{1/2}\sigma^{-1}_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}(y,a,w).

If
(A1)
holds, then the class of functions
\mathcal{H}_{h,b}:=\{\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
is VC type.

Proof of Corollary
5
.

For each
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
,
\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}
is defined as

\displaystyle h^{1/2}\sigma^{-1}_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\left[\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\xi_{\infty}(y,a,w)-\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)+\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\left\{\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},w)-\int\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},\bar{w})\,dQ_{0}(\bar{w})\right\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right].

Any collection of constants
\{c_{a_{0}}:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
is a VC class with VC index 1 because no set of size 2 can be shattered. This implies that
\{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0}):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
,
\{P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
,
\{\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}[i,j]:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
for each
(i,j)
, and
\{\iint\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},\bar{w})\,dQ_{0}(\bar{w})\,dF_{0}(\bar{a}):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
are all VC type. We note that by permanence properties of uniform covering numbers, sums and products of VC classes of functions are also VC type. It thus remains to show that
\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\xi_{\infty}(y,a,w):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
,
\{\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
and
\{\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},w)\,dF_{0}(\bar{a}):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
are VC-type.

We recall that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)-e_{3}^{T}c_{0,h,a_{,}2}(h/b)^{2}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a).

By Lemma
11
, each entry of
\{w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
and
\{w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
are VC type. Combined with the discussion of sets of constants above, this implies that
\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
is VC type for each
h,b>0
. By Lemma 2.6.18 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, element-wise products of a fixed function and a VC class of functions are also VC type. Hence, it follows that
\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\xi_{\infty}(y,a,w):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
is also VC type. Similarly,
\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{\infty}(a,w):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
is VC type. Lemma 5.2 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
(with
r=s=t=2
) then implies that
\{\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,s}(\bar{a})\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},w)\,dF_{0}(\bar{a}):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
is also VC type.

Finally, we recall that

\displaystyle\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)w^{T}_{h,s,1}(a)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-c_{2}(h/b)^{2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)w^{T}_{b,a_{0},2}(a)\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+(h/b)^{2}e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\left[\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)-w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}P_{0}(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})\right]K_{h,a_{0}}(a)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\times e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)

As in Lemma
11
, each entry of
\{w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)w^{T}_{h,a_{0},1}(a):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
,
\{w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)w^{T}_{b,a_{0},2}(a):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
, and
\{\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)w^{T}_{h,a_{0},1}(a):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
are VC type. Thus by the permanence property of VC-type function classes, we conclude that
\{\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
is also VC type.
∎

Lemma 12
.

If
(A1)
and
(A8)
hold, then
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}
and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\|_{\infty}
are
O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1/2})
. If in addition
nh/\log h^{-1}\longrightarrow\infty
, then
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\|_{\infty}
, and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}|
are
O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1/2})
.

Proof of Lemma
12
.

For all
1\leq j,k\leq 2
, we can write

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]\right|

\displaystyle=h^{-1}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\int\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)\,\left\{dF_{n}(a)-\,dF_{0}(a)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=n^{-1/2}h^{-1}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{j+k-2,h}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}f\right|

for

\mathcal{F}_{j+k-2,h}:=\left\{a\mapsto\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)\,:\,a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\right\}.

By
(A1)
and Lemma
11
,
\mathcal{F}_{j+k-2,h}
is a uniformly bounded VC class. For all
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left\{\left(\frac{A-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}K\left(\frac{A-a_{0}}{h}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq E_{0}\left[\left\{\left(\frac{A-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}K\left(\frac{A-a_{0}}{h}\right)\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\int\left\{\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle=\int hu^{2(j+k-2)}K(u)^{2}f_{0}(uh+a_{0})\,du

\displaystyle\leq h\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}c_{2(j+k-2)}.

Additionally, we have that
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j+k-2}K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)|\leq\|K\|_{\infty}
by
(A1)
. Hence,
\mathcal{F}_{j+k-2,h}
satisfies the conditions of Theorem 2.1 of
Giné and Guillou, 2002
with
\sigma^{2}
proportional to
h
, which implies that that

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{j+k-2,h}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}f\right|=O_{p}\left(\left\{h\log h^{-1}\right\}^{1/2}\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}[j,k]\right|=O_{p}\left(\left\{\frac{\log h^{-1}}{nh}\right\}^{1/2}\right)

for each
1\leq j,k\leq 2
. We conclude that
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}
and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},2}\|_{\infty}
are both
O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1/2})
. The results for the inverse matrices follow along the lines of Lemma
8
, where we use the assumption that
nh/\log h^{-1}\longrightarrow\infty
to conclude that
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\|_{\infty}=o_{p}(1)
.

Finally, we can write

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|e_{1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right)\right|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0}\right)\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\right|.

The first term is
O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1/2})
by the result for
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\|_{\infty}
, and the second term is
O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1/2})
by a similar calculation to that above.
∎

As above, we define

\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}(y,a,w):=h^{1/2}\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}K_{h,a_{0}}\left(a\right)\lambda(y,a,w),

and we let
\lambda_{\infty}
be a fixed function in
\Lambda
.
We also define the following semi-metric on
\Lambda
:

\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}):=\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}\left(E_{0}\left[\left\{\lambda_{1}(Y,A,W)-\lambda_{2}(Y,A,W)\right\}^{2}\mid A=a_{0}\right]\right)^{1/2},

where
\mathcal{A}_{\delta}:=\{a:\exists a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},|a-a_{0}|\leq\delta\}
. For each
r>0
, we then define
\Lambda_{r}:=\{\lambda\in\Lambda:\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}(\lambda,\lambda_{\infty})\leq r\}
and
\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}:=\{\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}-\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{\infty}}:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},\lambda\in\Lambda_{r}\}
. If
\Lambda
is equipped with an envelope function
L
, then
H_{h}:=2h^{-1/2}\|K\|_{\infty}L
is an envelope for
\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}
. The next Lemma controls the uniform entropy of
\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}
in terms of that of
\Lambda
.

Lemma 13
.

Suppose
\Lambda
has envelope function
L
with
PL^{2}<\infty
and such that
\sup_{Q}\log N(\varepsilon\|L\|_{Q,2},\Lambda,L_{2}(Q))\leq C\varepsilon^{-V}
for some
C,V<\infty
. If
(A1)
also holds, then

\sup_{Q}\log N(\varepsilon\|H_{h}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r},L_{2}(Q))\leq C^{\prime}\varepsilon^{-V}

for all
\varepsilon\leq 1
and some
C^{\prime}<\infty
.

Proof of Lemma
13
.

For each
h>0
and
j\in\{1,\dotsc,4\}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{h,j}:=\left\{\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\right\},

which is uniformly bounded by
\|K\|_{\infty}
. We have that
\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}
is then contained in the product of
h^{-1/2}\mathcal{K}_{h,j}
and
\Lambda-\lambda_{\infty}
, which has envelope
2L
. Lemma 5.1 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon\|H_{h,j,r}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r},L_{2}(Q)\right)\leq\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon h^{-1/2}\|K\|_{\infty},h^{-1/2}\mathcal{K}_{h,j},L_{2}(Q)\right)+\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon\|L\|_{Q,2},\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)

for any
\varepsilon>0
. By Lemma
11
,
\mathcal{K}_{h,j}
is VC type, which implies by Theorem 2.6.7 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
that

\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon h^{-1/2}\|K\|_{\infty},h^{-1/2}\mathcal{K}_{h,j},L_{2}(Q)\right)=\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon\|K\|_{\infty},\mathcal{K}_{h,j},L_{2}(Q)\right)\lesssim\log\varepsilon^{-1},

which is bounded up to a constant by
\varepsilon^{-V}
.
∎

Lemma 14
.

Suppose
\Lambda
is a class of functions uniformly bounded by
L<\infty
and satisfying
\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon,\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{-V}
for some
C<\infty
and
V\in(0,2)
such that
n\left[h/(\log n)\right]^{\frac{2+V}{2-V}}\longrightarrow\infty
. If
r=r_{n}>0
is a sequence satisfying

r=o\left(h^{\frac{V}{2(2-V)}}\{\log n\}^{-\frac{1}{2-V}}\right)

and
(A1)
holds, then

E_{0}\left[\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta\right|\right]=o(\{\log n\}^{-1/2})

for each
j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
. Consequently, if the above conditions hold and
\lambda_{n}\in\Lambda
is a sequence of possibly random functions satisfying

\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}(\lambda_{n},\lambda_{\infty})=o_{p}\left(h^{\frac{V}{2(2-V)}}\{\log n\}^{-\frac{1}{2-V}}\right)

for some
\delta>0
and
(A8)
also holds, then

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\lambda_{n}-\lambda_{\infty})\right\}\right|=o_{p}\left(\{\log n\}^{-1/2}\right).

Proof of Lemma
14
.

For each
\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}
such that
h\leq\delta
, we have

\displaystyle P_{0}(\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}-\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{\infty}})^{2}

\displaystyle=h^{-1}\int\left\{\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)(\lambda-\lambda_{\infty})(y,a,w)\right\}^{2}\,dP_{0}(y,a,w)

\displaystyle=h^{-1}\int\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{2(j-1)}\left\{K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)\right\}^{2}E_{0}\left\{(\lambda-\lambda_{\infty})^{2}\mid A=a\right\}f_{0}(a)\,da

\displaystyle=\int u^{2(j-1)}K(u)^{2}E_{0}\{(\lambda-\lambda_{\infty})^{2}\mid A=a_{0}+uh\}f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\leq\|K\|_{\infty}^{2}\sup_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}E_{0}\{(\lambda-\lambda_{\infty})^{2}\mid A=a\}\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|K\|_{\infty}^{2}\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}(\lambda,\lambda_{\infty})^{2}\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}.

Hence, for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}
with
h\leq\delta
, we have
P_{0}\zeta^{2}\leq r^{2}\|K\|_{\infty}^{2}\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}
. Since
\Lambda
is uniformly bounded by
L
,
\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}
is uniformly bounded by
H_{h,j,r}=2h^{-1/2}L\|K\|_{\infty}
, and hence
P_{0}H^{2}_{h,j,r}=4h^{-1}L^{2}\|K\|_{\infty}^{2}
. Therefore,
P_{0}\zeta^{2}\leq C^{2}hr^{2}P_{0}H^{2}_{h,j,r}
for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}
with
h\leq\delta
, where
C=\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{1/2}/(2L)
does not depend on
h
or
r
. Theorem 2.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 2011
(applied to
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}/H_{h,j,r}
, which is uniformly bounded by 1) then implies that for all
n
large enough,

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta|\right]\lesssim h^{-1/2}J(Crh^{1/2},\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r},L_{2})\left\{1+\frac{J(Crh^{1/2},\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r},L_{2})}{C^{2}hr^{2}n^{1/2}}\right\},

where

\displaystyle J(x,\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r},L_{2})

\displaystyle:=\sup_{Q}\int_{0}^{x}\sqrt{1+\log N\left(\varepsilon\|H_{h,j,r}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r},L_{2}(Q)\right)}\,d\varepsilon\lesssim\int_{0}^{x}\varepsilon^{-V/2}\,d\varepsilon\lesssim x^{1-V/2}

for all
x\in(0,1]
by Lemma
13
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\{\log n\}^{1/2}E_{0}\left[\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\{\log n\}^{1/2}h^{-1/2}r^{\frac{2-V}{2}}h^{\frac{2-V}{4}}\left\{1+\frac{r^{\frac{2-V}{2}}h^{\frac{2-V}{4}}}{hr^{2}n^{1/2}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{r\left[\log n\right]^{\frac{1}{2-V}}h^{-\frac{V}{2(2-V)}}\right\}^{\frac{2-V}{2}}+\left\{r\left[\log n\right]^{-\frac{1}{2V}}n^{\frac{1}{2V}}h^{\frac{1+V}{2V}}\right\}^{-V}

\displaystyle=\left\{rs\right\}^{\frac{2-V}{2}}+\left\{rt\right\}^{-V},

where
s:=\left[\log n\right]^{\frac{1}{2-V}}h^{-\frac{V}{2(2-V)}}
and
t:=\left[\log n\right]^{-\frac{1}{2V}}n^{\frac{1}{2V}}h^{\frac{1+V}{2V}}
. We note that

t/s=\left\{n\left[h/(\log n)\right]^{\frac{2+V}{2-V}}\right\}^{\frac{1}{2V}}\longrightarrow\infty

by assumption. We now define
\bar{r}:=\max\{r,[st]^{-1/2}\}
. Since
r\leq\bar{r}
, we then have

\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta|\leq\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,\bar{r}}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta|

almost surely by the increasing nature of the sets
\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}
. Using the above bound applied to
\bar{r}
, we also have

\displaystyle\{\log n\}^{1/2}E_{0}\left[\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,\bar{r}}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\{\bar{r}s\right\}^{\frac{2-V}{2}}+\left\{\bar{r}t\right\}^{-V},

By assumption,
rs=o(1)
, and
[st]^{-1/2}s=[s/t]^{1/2}
, which tends to zero since
t/s\longrightarrow\infty
as noted above. Therefore,
\bar{r}s=o(1)
. In addition,
\bar{r}t\geq[st]^{-1/2}t=[t/s]^{1/2}\longrightarrow\infty
. Hence,
\left\{\bar{r}s\right\}^{\frac{2-V}{2}}+\left\{\bar{r}t\right\}^{-V}=o(1)
. Putting it together, we have

\{\log n\}^{1/2}E_{0}\left[\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,r}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta|\right]\leq\{\log n\}^{1/2}E_{0}\left[\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,\bar{r}}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta|\right]\lesssim\left\{\bar{r}s\right\}^{\frac{2-V}{2}}+\left\{\bar{r}t\right\}^{-V}=o(1),

which proves the first claim.

For the second claim, as in the proof of Lemma
9
, we can write

h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\lambda=\sum_{j=1}^{2}C_{h,a_{0},j}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}C_{h,b,a_{0},j}^{\prime}\eta_{b,a_{0},j,\lambda}

for some constants
C_{h,a_{0},j}
and
C_{b,a_{0},j}
such that
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|C_{h,a_{0},j}|=O(1)
and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|C_{h,b,a_{0},j}^{\prime}|=O(1)
by the uniform statements of Lemma
4
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\lambda_{n}-\lambda_{\infty})\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|C_{h,a_{0},j}\right|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|C_{h,b,a_{0},j}^{\prime}\right|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{b,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|

\displaystyle=O(1)\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|+O(1)\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{b,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|.

Hence, if we can show that
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|=o_{p}\left(\{\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
for each
j
, then the claim follows.

For any
\nu,\gamma>0
, we have

\displaystyle P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|>\nu/\{\log n\}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|>\nu/\{\log n\}^{1/2},\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}(\lambda_{n},\lambda_{\infty})<\gamma/s\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+P_{0}\left(\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}(\lambda_{n},\lambda_{\infty})\geq\gamma/s\right).

Now,
\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}(\lambda_{n},\lambda_{\infty})<\gamma/s
implies that
\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,\gamma/s}
, so by Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|>\nu/\{\log n\}^{1/2},\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{0},\delta}(\lambda_{n},\lambda_{\infty})<\gamma/s\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}\left(\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,\gamma/a}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta\right|>\nu/\{\log n\}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\nu^{-1}\{\log n\}^{1/2}E_{0}\left[\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,\gamma/s}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta\right|\right].

Applying the same the same technique as used above, we then have

\displaystyle\{\log n\}^{1/2}E_{0}\left[\sup_{\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,j,\gamma/s}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\max\{\gamma/s(st)^{-1/2}\}s\right]^{\frac{2-V}{2}}+\left[\max\{\gamma/s,(st)^{-1/2}\}t\right]^{-V}

\displaystyle=\left[\max\{\gamma,(s/t)^{1/2}\}\right]^{\frac{2-V}{2}}+\left[\max\{\gamma(t/s),(t/s)^{1/2}\}\right]^{-V}.

Therefore, for any
\nu,\gamma>0
, we have

\displaystyle P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|>\nu/\{\log n\}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{-1}\left[\max\{\gamma,(s/t)^{1/2}\}\right]^{\frac{2-V}{2}}+\nu^{-1}\left[\max\{\gamma(t/s),(t/s)^{1/2}\}\right]^{-V}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+P_{0}\left(\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}(\lambda_{n},\lambda_{\infty})\geq\gamma/s\right)

Since
s/t\longrightarrow 0
,
\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta}}(\lambda_{n},\lambda_{\infty})=o_{p}(s^{-1})
, and
\gamma
was arbitrary, for any fixed
\nu>0
, we can choose
\gamma
to make the above expression as small as we like for all
n
large enough. This implies that

P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|>\nu/\{\log n\}^{1/2}\right)\longrightarrow 0

for any
\nu>0
, so that
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda_{n}}\right|=o_{p}\left(\{\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
for each
j\in\{1,2,\dots\}
, which completes the proof.

∎

Next, we define

\bar{\eta}_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}(y,a,w):=\int\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}(y,a,w)\,dF_{0}(a)=\int h^{1/2}\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)^{j-1}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\lambda(y,a,w)\,dF_{0}(a).

The next lemma provides a rate of convergence for
\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S},\lambda\in\Lambda}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\eta}_{h,s,j,\lambda}\right|
, which is sufficient to obtain a rate of convergence of
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,s,2}|
and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,s,3}|
. We note that we could obtain an even faster rate of convergence for
\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S},\lambda\in\Lambda}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\bar{\eta}_{h,s,j,\lambda}-\bar{\eta}_{h,s,j,\lambda_{\infty}}\right\}\right|
using similar techniques to those above for
\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S},\lambda\in\Lambda}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\eta_{h,s,j,\lambda}-\eta_{h,s,j,\lambda_{\infty}}\right\}\right|
. However, the integration over
a
in
\bar{\eta}_{h,s,j,\lambda}
is sufficient to obtain a rate of convergence without localizing around
\lambda_{\infty}
, which provides a sufficient rate for our purposes.

Lemma 15
.

Suppose
\Lambda
is a class of functions uniformly bounded by
L<\infty
and satisfying
\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon,\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{-V}
for some
C<\infty
and
V\in(0,2)
. If
(A1)
holds, then
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},\lambda\in\Lambda}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{\eta}_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}\right|=O_{p}\left(h^{\frac{1-V}{2}}+\left\{nh^{1+2V}\right\}^{-1/2}\right)
for every
j\in\{1,2,\dots\}
. Consequently, if
(A8)
holds as well, then

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},\lambda\in\Lambda}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\lambda\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|=O_{p}\left(h^{\frac{1-V}{2}}+\left\{nh^{1+2V}\right\}^{-1/2}\right).

Proof of Lemma
15
.

We consider the class of functions
\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,j}:=\{\bar{\eta}_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},\lambda\in\Lambda\}
. We equip this class with the envelope function
H_{h}=h^{-1/2}L\|K\|_{\infty}
. We also define
\mathcal{H}_{h,j}:=\{\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},\lambda\in\Lambda\}
, and we note that
H_{h}
is also an envelope for
\mathcal{H}_{h,j}
. Hence, by Lemma 5.2 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
with
s=t=r=2
and Lemma
13
, we have

\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon H_{h},\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,j},L_{2}(Q)\right)\leq\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon H_{h}/2,\mathcal{H}_{h,j},L_{2}(Q)\right)\lesssim\varepsilon^{-V}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle J(x,\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,j},L_{2})

\displaystyle:=\sup_{Q}\int_{0}^{x}\sqrt{1+\log N\left(\varepsilon\bar{H}_{h},\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,j},L_{2}(Q)\right)}\,d\varepsilon\lesssim x^{1-V/2}

for all
x\in(0,1]
. We also have

\displaystyle P_{0}\bar{\eta}_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq h^{-1}L^{2}\left\{\int\left|\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right|^{j-1}K\left(\frac{a-a_{0}}{h}\right)\,dF_{0}(a)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=hL^{2}\left\{\int\left|u\right|^{j-1}K\left(u\right)f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq hL^{2}\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|K\|_{\infty}^{2}

for every
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
and
\lambda\in\Lambda
. Hence,
P_{0}\bar{\eta}_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}^{2}\leq\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}h^{2}P_{0}H_{h}^{2}
for every
\bar{\eta}_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}\in\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,j}
. By Theorem 2.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 2011
, we then have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{\zeta\in\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,j}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\zeta|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{-1/2}J\left(\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}h,\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,j},L_{2}\right)\left\{1+\frac{J\left(\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}h,\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{h,j},L_{2}\right)}{n^{1/2}\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}h^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{\frac{1-V}{2}}\left\{1+n^{-1/2}h^{-\frac{2+V}{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle=h^{\frac{1-V}{2}}+\left\{nh^{1+2V}\right\}^{-1/2}.

The second claim follows as in the proof of Lemma
14
.

∎

Lemma 16
.

Suppose
\Lambda
is a class of functions uniformly bounded by
L<\infty
such that
\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon,\Lambda,L_{2}(Q)\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{-V}
for some
C<\infty
and
V\in(0,2)
. If
(A1)
holds, then for each
j\in\{1,2,\dots\}
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left|\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}\right|=O_{p}\left(h^{-1/2}\right)
.

Proof of Lemma
16
.

Since
L
is an envelope for
\Lambda
,
h^{-1/2}\|K\|_{\infty}L
is an envelope for
\{\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},\lambda\in\Lambda\}
by
(A1)
. By Lemma
13
, the uniform entropy integral of this class is finite. Hence, by Theorem 2.14.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
, we have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left|\eta_{h,a_{0},j,\lambda}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{-1/2},

and the result follows.
∎

Corollary 6
.

If
(A1)
–
(A3)
,
(A6)
,
(A7)
(d), and
(A8)
hold and
nh^{p}\longrightarrow 0
for some
p>0
, then
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}\right|=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},3}\right|=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
.

Proof of Corollary
6
.

As in the proof of Corollary
4
, we can write

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\{nh\}^{1/2}R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\left(\psi_{n}-\psi_{\infty}\right)\right\}\right|

(12)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\left(\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}-\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu_{\infty}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|.

(13)

For the first term, we use Lemma
14
with
\Lambda:=\{o\mapsto[y-\mu(a,w)]/g(a,w):\mu\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu},g\in\mathcal{F}_{g}\}
. By
(A3)
and
(A8)
,
\Lambda
is uniformly bounded by some
L<\infty
. By
(A3)
and permanence properties of uniform entropy integrals,
\sup_{Q}\log N(\varepsilon,\Lambda,L_{2}(Q))\leq C\varepsilon^{-V}
for
V=\max\{V_{\mu},V_{g}\}\in(0,2)
, and by
(A6)
,
n[h/(\log n)]^{\frac{2+V}{2-V}}\longrightarrow\infty
. Using a similar argument to that used in the proof of Corollary
4
and using the assumption that
|Y|
is bounded almost surely, we can show that

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}}(\psi_{n},\psi_{\infty})\lesssim d(g_{n},g_{\infty};\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}},\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W})+d(\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty};\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}},\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W}).

Hence, by
(A7)
(d),
\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}}(\psi_{n},\psi_{\infty})=o_{p}\left(h^{\frac{V}{2(2-V)}}\{\log n\}^{-\frac{1}{2-V}}\right)
. Thus, the conditions of Lemma
14
hold, and it follows that
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\psi_{n}-\psi_{\infty})\}\right|=o_{p}(\{\log n\}^{-1/2})
.

For the second term in equation (
13
), we also use Lemma
14
with
\Lambda=\left\{a\mapsto\int\mu(a,w)\,dQ_{0}(w):\mu\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}\right\}
. This class is uniformly bounded since
\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
is uniformly bounded by
(A3)
. By
(A3)
and Lemma 5.2 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
,
\sup_{Q}\log N(\varepsilon,\Lambda,L_{2}(Q))\lesssim\varepsilon^{-V_{\mu}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-V}
, and by
(A6)

n[h/(\log n)]^{\frac{2+V}{2-V}}\longrightarrow\infty
. By Jensen’s inequality and
(A7)
(d),

\rho_{\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}}}\left(\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0},\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)\leq d(\mu_{n},\mu_{\infty};\mathcal{A}_{\delta_{3}},\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W})=o_{p}\left(h^{\frac{V}{2(2-V)}}\{\log n\}^{-\frac{1}{2-V}}\right).

The conditions of Lemma
14
are satisfied, and it follows that

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\left(\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}-\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right)\right\}\right|=o_{p}\left(\{\log n\}^{-1/2}\right).

For the third and fourth terms in equation (
13
), we use Lemma
15
with
\Lambda=\mathcal{F}_{\mu}
. The conditions of the lemma are satisfied by
(A3)
, so that

\{\log n\}^{1/2}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},\mu\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|=O_{p}\left(\{\log n\}^{1/2}h^{\frac{1-V_{\mu}}{2}}+\left\{nh^{1+2V_{\mu}}/\log n\right\}^{-1/2}\right).

By
(A6)
,
nh^{3}\longrightarrow\infty
, which implies that
nh^{1+2V_{\mu}}/\log n\longrightarrow\infty
since
V_{\mu}\in(0,1)
. By assumption,
nh^{p}\longrightarrow 0
for some
p>0
, and since
V_{\mu}\in(0,1)
, this implies that
\{\log n\}^{1/2}h^{\frac{1-V_{\mu}}{2}}=o(1)
as well. Hence,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0},\mu\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\mu\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|=o_{p}\left(\{\log n\}^{-1/2}\right).

We have now shown that every term of equation (
13
) is
o_{p}\left(\{\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
, so we conclude that
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}\right|=o_{p}\left(\left\{nh\log n\right\}^{-1/2}\right)
.

We can similarly decompose
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},3}
as

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|(nh)^{1/2}R_{n,h,b,a_{0},3}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\psi_{n}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\int\mu_{n}\,dQ_{0}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|.

(14)

For the first term in equation (
14
), we note that

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\psi_{n}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|e_{1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right)\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}(h/b)^{2}e_{3}^{T}\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right)\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\left.-(c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2})(h/b)^{2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{h,a_{0},1}\mathbf{1}^{T}K_{h,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},2}\mathbf{1}^{T}K_{b,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0}2}\right|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},2}\mathbf{1}^{T}K_{b,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}\right|.

By
(A6)
,
nh^{3}\longrightarrow\infty
, which implies that
nh/\log h^{-1}\longrightarrow\infty
. Hence, by Lemma
12
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}
, and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0}2}\right|
are all
O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1/2})
. In addition, by Lemma
16
, we have that
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{h,a_{0},1}^{T}\mathbf{1}K_{h,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}\right|
and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},2}^{T}\mathbf{1}K_{b,a_{0}}\psi_{n}\right\}\right|
are both
O_{p}(h^{-1/2})
. We then have

\{\log n\}^{1/2}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\psi_{n}\right\}\right|=O_{p}\left(\left\{\frac{nh^{2}}{\log h^{-1}\log n}\right\}^{-1/2}\right).

Since
nh^{3}\longrightarrow\infty
and
nh^{p}\longrightarrow 0
for some
p>0
,
\frac{nh^{2}}{\log h^{-1}\log n}\longrightarrow\infty
. The second term in equation (
14
) can be addressed in the same way using Lemma 5.2 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
.

We can similarly bound the third term in equation (
14
) up to a constant by

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}w_{h,a_{0},1}^{T}\mathbf{1}K_{h,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},2}^{T}\mathbf{1}K_{b,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\right|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}w_{b,a_{0},2}^{T}\mathbf{1}K_{b,a_{0}}\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|

By Lemmas
12
and
15
, we then have

\displaystyle\{\log n\}^{1/2}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left\{\int h^{1/2}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right)\mu_{n}\,dF_{0}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=O_{p}\left(\left\{\frac{nh}{\log h^{-1}\log n}\right\}^{-1/2}\left\{h^{(1-V_{\mu})/2}+\left(nh^{1+2V_{\mu}}\right)^{-1/2}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=O_{p}\left(\left\{\frac{nh^{V_{\mu}}}{\log h^{-1}\log n}\right\}^{-1/2}+\left\{\frac{nh^{1+V_{\mu}}}{\left(\log h^{-1}\log n\right)^{1/2}}\right\}^{-1}\right).

By
(A6)
, both terms are
o(1)
.
∎

Supplement I
Analysis of remainder term
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}

Lemma 17
.

If
(A1)
–
(A4)
hold, then
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}=o_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})
. If
(A1)
–
(A3)
,
(A7)
, and
(A8)
hold, then
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}\right|=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
.

Proof of Lemma
17
.

We note that
\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)=0
for all
a
such that
|a-a_{0}|>\max\{h,b\}
by
(A1)
. Hence, for
\max\{h,b\}\leq\delta_{1}
,
\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)=0
for
a\notin B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})
. Therefore, using
(A4)
, for all
\max\{h,b\}\leq\delta_{1}
, we can write

\displaystyle\left|\mathrel{R}_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\iint_{B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0})\times\mathcal{W}}\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\left\{\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{0}(a,w)\right\}\left\{1-\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{n}(a,w)}\right\}\,dF_{0}(a)\,dQ_{0}(w)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{j=1}^{3}\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\left\{\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{0}(a,w)\right\}\left\{1-\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{n}(a,w)}\right\}\,dF_{0}(a)\,dQ_{0}(w)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{3}\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}\left|\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\right|\left|\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{0}(a,w)\right|\left|1-\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{n}(a,w)}\right|\,dF_{0}(a)\,dQ_{0}(w).

Now we note that for any uniformly bounded function
\lambda:\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{W}
, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{S}\left|w_{h,a_{0},j}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\lambda(a,w)\right|\,dF_{0}(a)\,dQ_{0}(w)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\int_{-1}^{1}\left|(1,u,\dotsc,u^{j})^{T}\right|K(u)\int I_{\mathcal{S}}(a_{0}+uh,w)\left|\lambda(a_{0}+uh,w)\right|\,dQ_{0}(w)f_{0}(a_{0}+uh)\,du

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\lesssim\sup_{|a-a_{0}|\leq h}\int I_{\mathcal{S}}(a,w)\left|\lambda(a,w)\right|\,dQ_{0}(w).

Hence, for all
n
large enough, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}\left|\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\right|\left|\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{0}(a,w)\right|\left|1-\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{n}(a,w)}\right|\,dF_{0}(a)\,dQ_{0}(w)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\lesssim\left[\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\right]\sup_{|a-a_{0}|<\delta_{1}}E_{0}\left\{I_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}(a,W)\left|\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{0}(a,w)\right|\left|1-\frac{g_{0}(a,W)}{g_{n}(a,W)}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\lesssim O_{p}(1)d\left(\mu_{n},\mu_{0};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{j}\right)d\left(g_{n},g_{0};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{j}\right),

where for the last inequality we used Lemma
8
, the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, and the uniform boundedness of
1/g_{n}
guaranteed by
(A3)
. We now use
(A4)
to see that

\displaystyle d\left(\mu_{n},\mu_{0};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right),

\displaystyle d\left(g_{n},g_{0};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right),\text{ and}

\displaystyle d\left(\mu_{n},\mu_{0};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{3}\right)d\left(g_{n},g_{0};B_{\delta_{1}}(a_{0}),\mathcal{S}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right).

Thus,
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}=o_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\right)
.

For the uniform statement, we note that
\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)=0
for all
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
,
a\notin\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{3}}
, and
\max\{h,b\}<\varepsilon_{3}
. Hence, for
\max\{h,b\}<\varepsilon_{3}
, we can write

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\iint_{\mathcal{A}_{3}\times\mathcal{W}}\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\left\{\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{0}(a,w)\right\}\left\{1-\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{n}(a,w)}\right\}\,dF_{0}(a)\,dQ_{0}(w)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{j}^{\prime}}\left|\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\right|\left|\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{0}(a,w)\right|\left|1-\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{n}(a,w)}\right|\,dF_{0}(a)\,dQ_{0}(w).

Using the derivations above, we then have

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\iint_{\mathcal{S}_{j}^{\prime}}\left|\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\right|\left|\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{0}(a,w)\right|\left|1-\frac{g_{0}(a,w)}{g_{n}(a,w)}\right|\,dF_{0}(a)\,dQ_{0}(w)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\lesssim\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left[\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\right]\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{3}}}E_{0}\left\{I_{\mathcal{S}_{j}^{\prime}}(a,W)\left|\mu_{n}(a,w)-\mu_{0}(a,w)\right|\left|1-\frac{g_{0}(a,W)}{g_{n}(a,W)}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\lesssim O_{p}(1)d\left(\mu_{n},\mu_{0};\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{3}},\mathcal{S}_{j}^{\prime}\right)d\left(g_{n},g_{0};\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{3}},\mathcal{S}_{j}^{\prime}\right),

where the last inequality uses Lemma
12
and the uniform boundedness of
1/g_{n}
guaranteed by
(A3)
. We now use the faster rates guaranteed by
(A7)
to see that

\displaystyle d(\mu_{n},\mu_{0};\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{3}},\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right),

\displaystyle d(g_{n},g_{0};\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{3}},\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right),\text{ and}

\displaystyle d(\mu_{n},\mu_{0};\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{3}},\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\prime})d_{2}(g_{n},g_{0};\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon_{3}},\mathcal{S}_{3}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right).

Thus,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}\right|=o_{p}\left(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2}\right)
.
∎

Supplement J
Analysis of remainder term
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}

Lemma 18
.

Suppose
X_{1},X_{2},\dots
is a sequence of IID random variables on sample space
\mathcal{X}
with marginal distribution
P_{0}
. Let
\mathbb{P}_{n}
be the empirical distribution corresponding to
(X_{1},\dotsc,X_{n})
, and
\mathcal{F}
be a collection of measurable functions from
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}
to
\mathbb{R}
with envelope function
F
. Then

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\iint f(x_{1},x_{2})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-1}\left\|F\right\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}\int_{0}^{1}\left[1+\log\sup_{Q}N(\varepsilon\|F\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q))\right]\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+n^{-3/2}\|F\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}\int_{0}^{1}\left[1+\log\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|F\|_{Q,2}/2,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q)\right)\right]^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon.

Proof of Lemma
18
.

For each
f\in\mathcal{F}
, define the symmetrized, centered version of
f
as

\displaystyle f^{\circ}(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle:=f(x_{1},x_{2})+f(x_{2},x_{1})-\int\left[f(u,x_{1})+f(u,x_{2})+f(x_{1},u)+f(x_{2},u)\right]\,dP_{0}(u)+2\iint f(u,v)\,dP_{0}(u)\,dP_{0}(v).

We note that
f^{\circ}
is symmetric in its arguments, meaning
f^{\circ}(x_{1},x_{2})=f^{\circ}(x_{2},x_{1})
for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathcal{X}
, and
\int f(x_{1},u)\,dP_{0}(u)=0
for all
x_{1}\in\mathcal{X}
. We let
\mathcal{F}^{\circ}:=\{f^{\circ}:f\in\mathcal{F}\}
, and we note that an envelope function for
\mathcal{F}^{\circ}
is
F^{\circ}
for

\displaystyle F^{\circ}(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle:=F(x_{1},x_{2})+F(x_{2},x_{1})+\int\left[F(u,x_{1})+F(u,x_{2})+F(x_{1},u)+F(x_{2},u)\right]\,dP_{0}(u)+2\iint F(u,v)\,dP_{0}(u)\,dP_{0}(v).

By adding and subtracting terms, we can write

\displaystyle\iint f(x_{1},x_{2})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{n}f^{\circ}(X_{i},X_{j})-\frac{1}{n}\iint f(x_{1},x_{2})\,dP_{0}(x_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x_{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\frac{1}{n}\iint f(x_{1},x_{2})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x_{1})\,dP_{0}(x_{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-\frac{1}{n}\iint f(x_{1},x_{2})\,dP_{0}(x_{1})\,dP_{0}(x_{2})

By Lemma 3 of
Westling et al., 2020
, we have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}^{\circ}}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{n}f^{\circ}(X_{i},X_{j})\right|\right]\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\left\|F^{\circ}\right\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}\int_{0}^{1}\left[1+\log\sup_{Q}N(\varepsilon\|F^{\circ}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F}^{\circ},L_{2}(Q))\right]\,d\varepsilon.

By the triangle inequality and Jensen’s inequality,
\left\|F^{\circ}\right\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}\leq 8\left\|F\right\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}
. We also note that by definition of
f^{\circ}
,
\mathcal{F}^{\circ}
is contained in the sum of
\mathcal{F}
and the following classes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{r}

\displaystyle:=\left\{(x_{1},x_{2})\mapsto f(x_{2},x_{1}):f\in\mathcal{F}\right\},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{11}

\displaystyle:=\left\{x_{1}\mapsto-\int f(x_{1},u)\,dP_{0}(u):f\in\mathcal{F}\right\},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{12}

\displaystyle:=\left\{x_{1}\mapsto-\int f(u,x_{1})\,dP_{0}(u):f\in\mathcal{F}\right\},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{21}

\displaystyle:=\left\{x_{2}\mapsto-\int f(x_{2},u)\,dP_{0}(u):f\in\mathcal{F}\right\},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{22}

\displaystyle:=\left\{x_{2}\mapsto-\int f(u,x_{2})\,dP_{0}(u):f\in\mathcal{F}\right\},

\displaystyle\text{and }\mathcal{F}_{m}

\displaystyle:=\left\{2\iint f(u,v)\,dP_{0}(u)\,dP_{0}(v):f\in\mathcal{F}\right\}.

By Lemma 5.1 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
,
\log\sup_{Q}N(\varepsilon\|F^{\circ}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F}^{\circ},L_{2}(Q))
is bounded by the sum of the uniform entropies of each of the above classes. The uniform entropy of
\mathcal{F}_{r}
is the same as that of
\mathcal{F}
because for any measure
Q
on
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}
,

\int[f_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})-f_{2}(x_{1},x_{2})]^{2}\,dQ(x_{1},x_{2})=\int[f_{1}(x_{2},x_{1})-f_{2}(x_{2},x_{1})]^{2}\,dQ_{r}(x_{1},x_{2}),

where
Q_{r}
is defined as
dQ_{r}(x_{1},x_{2}):=dQ(x_{2},x_{1})
. We equip
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{11}
with the envelope function

\bar{F}_{11}:x_{1}\mapsto\left[\int F(x_{1},u)^{2}\,dP_{0}(u)\right]^{1/2}.

We note that
\|\bar{F}_{11}\|_{P_{0},2}=\|F\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}
. By Lemma 5.2 of
van der Vaart and van der Laan, 2006
(with
r=s=t=2
), we have

\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|\bar{F}_{11}\|_{Q,2},\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{11},L_{2}(Q)\right)\leq\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|F\|_{Q,2}/2,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q)\right).

Identical results hold for
\bar{F}_{12}
,
\bar{F}_{21}
, and
\bar{F}_{22}
. For
\mathcal{F}_{m}
equipped with envelope
\|F\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}
, we have

\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|F\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2},\mathcal{F}_{m},L_{2}(Q)\right)\leq\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|F\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q)\right)

since

\left\|\int f_{1}\,d(P_{0}\times P_{0})-\int f_{2}\,d(P_{0}\times P_{0})\right\|_{Q,2}=\left|\int f_{1}\,d(P_{0}\times P_{0})-\int f_{2}\,d(P_{0}\times P_{0})\right|\leq\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}

for any probability measure
Q
. Therefore, we have
