\displaystyle\left\|F^{\circ}\right\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}\int_{0}^{1}\left[1+\log\sup_{Q}N(\varepsilon\|F^{\circ}\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F}^{\circ},L_{2}(Q))\right]\,d\varepsilon\lesssim\left\|F\right\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}\int_{0}^{1}\left[1+\log\sup_{Q}N(\varepsilon\|F\|_{Q,2},\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q))\right]\,d\varepsilon

Next, we have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}n^{-1}\left|\iint f(x_{1},x_{2})\,dP_{0}(x_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x_{2})\right|\right]

\displaystyle=n^{-3/2}E_{0}\left[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{22}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{f}\right|\right].

By Theorem 2.14.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
,

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{\bar{f}\in\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{22}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\bar{f}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bar{F}_{22}\|_{P_{0},2}\int_{0}^{1}\left[1+\log\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|\bar{F}_{22}\|_{Q,2},\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{22},L_{2}(Q)\right)\right]^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon.

Hence,

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}n^{-1}\left|\iint f(x_{1},x_{2})\,dP_{0}(x_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x_{2})\right|\right]\lesssim n^{-3/2}\|F\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}\int_{0}^{1}\left[1+\log\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|F\|_{Q,2}/2,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q)\right)\right]^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon.

By an identical argument, we also have

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}n^{-1}\left|\iint f(x_{1},x_{2})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(x_{1})\,dP_{0}(x_{2})\right|\right]\lesssim n^{-3/2}\|F\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}\int_{0}^{1}\left[1+\log\sup_{Q}N\left(\varepsilon\|F\|_{Q,2}/2,\mathcal{F},L_{2}(Q)\right)\right]^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon.

Finally,

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}n^{-1}\left|\iint f(x_{1},x_{2})\,dP_{0}(x_{1})\,dP_{0}(x_{2})\right|\leq n^{-1}\|F\|_{P_{0}\times P_{0},2}.

Putting together the pieces yields the result.
∎

Lemma 19
.

If
(A1)
–
(A3)
, and
(A5)
hold, then
\mathrel{R}_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}=O_{p}(\{nh^{1/2}\}^{-1})
. If
(A1)
,
(A3)
, and
(A8)
hold and
nh/\log h^{-1}\longrightarrow\infty
, then
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}|=O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1})
.

Proof of Lemma
19
.

For the pointwise statement, we write

\displaystyle\mathrel{R}_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}

\displaystyle=\iint\Gamma_{n,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{n}(a,w)\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})(w)\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})(a)

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\iint w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{n}(a,w)\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})(w)\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})(a)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-c_{n,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\iint w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{n}(a,w)\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})(w)\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})(a).

By Lemmas
4
and
8
,
\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}
and
\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}
are both
O_{p}(1)
. Hence, there exist constants
C_{n,j}=O_{p}(1)
such that

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{4}C_{n,j}\left|\iint[(a-a_{0})/h]^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{n}(a,w)\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})(w)\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})(a)\right|.

For each
j
, we then write

\displaystyle\left|\iint[(a-a_{0})/h]^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{n}(a,w)\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})(w)\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})(a)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\left|\iint f_{n,h,a_{0},j}(a_{1},w_{1},a_{2},w_{2})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{1},w_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{2},w_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{h,a_{0},j}}\left|\iint f(a_{1},w_{1},a_{2},w_{2})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{1},w_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{2},w_{2})\right|,

where

\displaystyle f_{n,h,a_{0},j}(a_{1},w_{1},a_{2},w_{2})

\displaystyle:=[(a_{1}-a_{0})/h]^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(a_{1})\mu_{n}(a_{1},w_{2})\text{ and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h,a_{0},j}

\displaystyle=\left\{(a_{1},w_{1},a_{2},w_{2})\mapsto[(a_{1}-a_{0})/h]^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(a_{1})\mu(a_{1},w_{2}):\mu\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu}\right\}.

By
(A1)
and
(A3)
, an envelope function
F_{h,a_{0}}
for
\mathcal{F}_{h,a_{0},j}
is given by a constant times
K_{h,a_{0}}(a_{1})
, and
\|F_{h,a_{0}}\|_{(P_{0}\times P_{0}),2}\lesssim h^{-1/2}
by the standard change of variables. In addition, by
(A3)
,

\sup_{Q}\log N\left(\varepsilon\|F_{h,a_{0}}\|_{(P_{0}\times P_{0}),2},\mathcal{F}_{h,a_{0},j},L_{2}(Q)\right)\lesssim\varepsilon^{-V_{\mu}},

where
V_{\mu}\in(0,1)
. Hence, by Lemma
18
,

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{h,a_{0},j}}\left|\iint f(a_{1},w_{1},a_{2},w_{2})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{1},w_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{2},w_{2})\right|\right]\lesssim n^{-1}h^{-1/2}

for each
j
, and the pointwise result follows.

For the uniform result, by Lemmas
4
and
12
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}
and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}
are both
O_{p}(1)
. Hence, there exist constants
C_{n,j}^{\prime}=O_{p}(1)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{2}C_{n,j}^{\prime}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\iint[(a-a_{0})/h]^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{n}(a,w)\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})(w)\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})(a)\right|.

As above, for each
j
we then write

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\iint[(a-a_{0})/h]^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\mu_{n}(a,w)\,d(Q_{n}-Q_{0})(w)\,d(F_{n}-F_{0})(a)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\iint f_{n,h,a_{0},j}(a_{1},w_{1},a_{2},w_{2})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{1},w_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{2},w_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{h,j}}\left|\iint f(a_{1},w_{1},a_{2},w_{2})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{1},w_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{2},w_{2})\right|,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h,j}

\displaystyle=\left\{(a_{1},w_{1},a_{2},w_{2})\mapsto[(a_{1}-a_{0})/h]^{j}K_{h,a_{0}}(a_{1})\mu(a_{1},w_{2}):\mu\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu},a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\right\}.

By
(A1)
and
(A3)
,
\mathcal{F}_{h,j}
is uniformly bounded up to a constant by
h^{-1}
, and
by Lemma
13
and
(A3)
,
\mathcal{F}_{h,j}
has uniform entropy bounded up to a constant by
\varepsilon^{-V_{\mu}}
relative to this envelope. Thus, by Lemma
18
,

\displaystyle E_{0}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{h,j}}\left|\iint f(a_{1},w_{1},a_{2},w_{2})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{1},w_{1})\,d(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(a_{2},w_{2})\right|\right]\lesssim(nh)^{-1}

for each
j
, and the result follows.
∎

Supplement K
Analysis of remainder term
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},6}

Lemma 20
.

If
(A1)
,
(A2)
, and
(A5)
hold, then
|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},6}|=O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1})
. If
(A1)
,
(A2)
, and
(A8)
hold and
nh/\log h^{-1}\longrightarrow\infty
, then
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},6}|=O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1})
.

Proof of Lemma
20
.

We note that by
(A1)
and
(A5)
,
\left|P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|
,
\left|P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|
, and
\left|P_{0}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},2}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\right|
are
O(1)
, and by Lemma
4
,
\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\right\|_{\infty}
and
\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\right\|_{\infty}
are also
O(1)
. Hence,

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},6}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left\|\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}_{n,b,a_{0},2}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P_{0})(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}-\mathbf{D}_{n,h,a_{0},1}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left|c_{n,h,a_{0},2}-c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\right|\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{n,b,a_{0},2}-\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\right\|_{\infty}

By Lemma
8
, each of the differences is
O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})
. Thus,
R_{n,h,b,a_{0},6}=O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1})
. For the uniform statement, by
(A1)
and
(A8)
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|
, and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|P_{0}\left(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},2}K_{h,a_{0}}\right)\right|
are all
O(1)
. By Lemma
4
and
(A8)
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\right\|_{\infty}
and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\|\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,b,a_{0},2}\right\|_{\infty}
are also
O(1)
. By Lemma
12
, the differences are all
O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1/2})
uniformly over
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
. We thus conclude
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},6}\right|=O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1})
.
∎

Supplement L
Analysis of the covariance estimator

Lemma 21
.

If
(A1)
–
(A5)
hold for
a_{0}=u
and
a_{0}=v
, then

\displaystyle h\mathbb{P}_{n}(\phi_{n,h,b,u}^{*}\phi_{n,h,b,v}^{*})-hP_{0}(\phi_{\infty,h,b,u}^{*}\phi_{\infty,h,b,v}^{*})=o_{p}(1).

If
(A1)
–
(A3)
and
(A6)
–
(A8)
hold and
nh^{5}=O(1)
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|h\mathbb{P}_{n}(\phi_{n,h,b,u}^{*}\phi_{n,h,b,v}^{*})-hP_{0}(\phi_{\infty,h,b,u}^{*}\phi_{\infty,h,b,v}^{*})\right|=O_{p}(n^{-p})

for some
p>0
.

Proof of Lemma
21
.

Analysis of this expression uses many of the techniques developed throughout this document. Hence, for brevity, we omit some of the details in this proof.

For convenience, we define

\displaystyle\eta_{n,h,b,u}(w)

\displaystyle:=\int\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}(a)\left[\mu_{n}(a,w)-\smallint\mu_{n}\,dQ_{n}\right]\,dF_{n}(a),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\eta_{0,h,b,u}(w)

\displaystyle:=\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}(a)\left[\mu_{\infty}(a,w)-\smallint\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right]\,dF_{0}(a).

Up to terms with
u
and
v
swapped, we then expand
h\mathbb{P}_{n}(\phi_{n,h,b,u}^{*}\phi_{n,h,b,v}^{*})-hP_{0}(\phi_{\infty,h,b,u}^{*}\phi_{\infty,h,b,v}^{*})
as

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(h\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\Gamma_{n,h,b,v}\xi_{n}^{2}\right)+n^{-1/2}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(h\gamma_{n,h,b,u}\gamma_{n,h,b,v}\right)-n^{-1/2}\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(h\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\gamma_{n,h,b,v}\xi_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+P_{0}\left[h\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}\right)\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,v}\xi_{n}-\gamma_{n,h,b,v}\right)\xi_{n}\right]+P_{0}\left[h\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}\left\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,v}\left(\xi_{n}+\xi_{\infty}\right)-\gamma_{0,h,b,v}\right\}\left\{\xi_{n}-\xi_{\infty}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-P_{0}\left[h\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}\xi_{n}-\gamma_{0,h,b,u}\right)\left(\gamma_{n,h,b,v}-\gamma_{0,h,b,v}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(h\eta_{n,h,b,u}\eta_{n,h,b,v}\right)+\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(h\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\eta_{n,h,b,v}\xi_{n}\right)-\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(h\gamma_{n,h,b,u}\eta_{n,h,b,v}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-P_{0}\left(h\eta_{0,h,b,u}\eta_{0,h,b,v}\right)-P_{0}\left(h\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}\eta_{0,h,b,v}\xi_{\infty}\right)+P_{0}\left(h\gamma_{0,h,b,u}\eta_{0,h,b,v}\right).

(15)

For the first term in (
15
), by expanding
\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\Gamma_{n,h,b,v}
and Lemmas
4
and
8
,
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(h\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\Gamma_{n,h,b,v}\xi_{n}^{2}\right)
can be decomposed into
O_{p}(1)
times terms of the form

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(hw_{h,u,j}w_{h,v,k}K_{h,u}K_{b,v}\xi_{n}^{2}\right).

By the bounded fourth moment of
Y
and condition
(A3)
, the class

\left\{hw_{h,u,j}w_{h,v,k}K_{h,u}K_{b,v}\xi_{n}^{2}:\mu_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu},g_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{g}\right\}

has finite uniform entropy integral and an envelope that is a square-integrable function times
h^{-1}
. Hence, by Theorem 2.14.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
,
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(h\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\Gamma_{n,h,b,v}\xi_{n}^{2}\right)=O_{p}(h^{-1})
. Similarly, by conditions
(A1)
and
(A3)
, the class

\left\{hw_{h,u,j}w_{h,v,k}K_{h,u}K_{b,v}\xi_{n}^{2}:\mu_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu},g_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{g},u\in\mathcal{A}_{0},v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\right\}

possesses finite uniform entropy integral and under
(A8)
, an envelope that is bounded up to a constant by
h^{-1}
. Therefore, by Lemmas
4
and
12
,
\sup_{u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(h\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\Gamma_{n,h,b,v}\xi_{n}^{2}\right)|=O_{p}(h^{-1})
as well.
Using an analogous argument, we can show that
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(h\gamma_{n,h,b,u}\gamma_{n,h,b,v}\right)
and
\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(h\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\gamma_{h,h,b,v}\xi_{n}\right)
, which are the second and third terms in (
15
) are
O_{p}(h^{-1})
both pointwise and uniformly.

The fourth term in (
15
) can be written as a constant times a sum of terms of the form

h\left(\mathbf{D}_{n,h,u,j}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,j}^{-1}\right)P_{0}\left[w_{h,u,k}K_{h,u}\left(\Gamma_{n,h,b,v}\xi_{n}-\gamma_{n,h,b,v}\right)\xi_{n}\right].

By Lemma
8
,
\|\mathbf{D}_{n,h,u,j}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,j}^{-1}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})
. Using a change of variables, we can also show that
\|w_{h,u,k}K_{h,u}\xi_{n}\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}=O_{p}(h^{-1/2})
and
\|\Gamma_{n,h,b,v}\xi_{n}-\gamma_{n,h,b,v}\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}=O_{p}(h^{-1/2})
. Hence, under the pointwise conditions this term is
O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1/2})=o_{p}(1)
. Under the uniform results, the same rates hold except that Lemma
12
includes an extra
\log h^{-1}
term. Hence, under the uniform conditions, this term is
O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1/2})=O_{p}(h)
uniformly over
u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
. A similar analysis applies to the sixth term in (
15
).

For the fifth term in (
15
), we can show that
\|\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}(\xi_{n}-\xi_{\infty})\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}=o_{p}(h^{-1/2})
and
\|\Gamma_{0,h,b,v}\left(\xi_{n}+\xi_{\infty}\right)-\gamma_{0,h,b,v}\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}=O_{p}(h^{-1/2})
, so the pointwise result follows by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. For the uniform result, by
(A6)
(e),

\sup_{u\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}(\xi_{n}-\xi_{\infty})\|_{L_{2}(P_{0})}=o_{p}\left(h^{-1/2}h^{\frac{V}{2(2-V)}}\{\log n\}^{-\frac{1}{2-V}}\right),

so we obtain a uniform rate of
o_{p}\left(h^{\frac{V}{2(2-V)}}\{\log n\}^{-\frac{1}{2-V}}\right)
for this term.

For the seventh term in (
15
), by the boundedness of
\mu_{n}
we have for any
u,v

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(h\eta_{n,h,b,u}\eta_{n,h,b,v}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h\int\left|\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\right|\,dF_{n}\int\left|\Gamma_{n,h,b,v}\right|\,dF_{n}.

As in the proof of Lemmas
8
and
12
, we can show that each of these terms is
O_{p}(1)
pointwise and uniformly under the appropriate conditions. The same holds when
\mathbb{P}_{n}
is replaced with
P_{0}
above. Similarly, for the eighth term in (
15
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(h\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\eta_{n,h,b,v}\xi_{n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h\mathbb{P}_{n}\left|\Gamma_{n,h,b,u}\xi_{n}\right|\int\left|\Gamma_{n,h,b,v}\right|\,dF_{n}.

We can show that both terms are
O_{p}(1)
pointwise and uniformly. The remainder of the terms in (
15
) can be handled using a similar argument.

We have now shown that

\displaystyle hP_{0}\left[\left(\phi_{n,h,b,u}^{*}-\phi_{\infty,h,b,u}^{*}\right)\phi_{n,h,b,v}^{*}\right]=o_{p}(1)

under the pointwise conditions, and

\displaystyle\sup_{u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}h\left|P_{0}\left[\left(\phi_{n,h,b,u}^{*}-\phi_{\infty,h,b,u}^{*}\right)\phi_{n,h,b,v}^{*}\right]\right|=o_{p}\left(n^{-p}\right)

for some
p>0
since
nh^{5}=O(1)
.

∎

Supplement M
Lemmas supporting uniform result

We now present a series of supporting lemmas towards the construction of uniform bands for
\theta_{0}(a_{0})
.

Lemma 22
.

Let
\sigma^{2}_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0}):=hP_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}})^{2}
. If
(A1)
–
(A2)
,
(A7)
(a), and
(A8)
hold, then there exist constants
c,C\in(0,\infty)
such that
c\leq\sigma^{2}_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\leq C
for all
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
and all h small enough.

Proof of Lemma
22
.

We note that
(A8)
implies that
(A5)
holds for all
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
, and
(A7)
(a) implies that
(A4)
(a) holds for all
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
. Therefore, the conditions of Lemma
5
are met for every
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
, so
\sigma_{\infty,h,b}^{2}(a_{0})
converges to
V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})/f_{0}(a_{0})
for each
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
. Since
\mathcal{A}_{0}
is compact, this convergence is also uniform in
a_{0}
. By Lemma
5
,
V_{K,\tau}\in(0,\infty)
, so by
(A8)
, there exist
c,C\in(0,\infty)
such that
c\leq V_{K,\tau}\sigma_{0}^{2}(a_{0})/f_{0}(a_{0})\leq C
for all
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
. Hence, for all
h
small enough,
c\leq\sigma_{\infty,h,b}^{2}(a_{0})\leq C
for all
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
.
∎

Lemma 23
.

If
(A1)
,
(A7)
(a), and
(A8)
hold, then
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}|h\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}|^{k}\lesssim h
for all
h
small enough.

Proof of Lemma
23
.

First by the triangle inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left[P_{0}|h\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}|^{k}\right]^{1/k}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left[P_{0}\left|h\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\xi_{\infty}\right|^{k}\right]^{1/k}+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left[P_{0}\left|h\gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\right|^{k}\right]^{1/k}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left[P_{0}\left|h\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\left\{\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},w)-\int\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},\bar{w})\,dQ_{0}(\bar{w})\right\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right|^{k}\right]^{1/k}.

(16)

We show that three terms on the right hand side of the above display are bounded up to a constant by
h^{1/k}
, which implies that
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}P_{0}|h\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}|^{k}
is bounded up to a constant by
h
. Noting that
|\xi_{\infty}|
is
P_{0}
-almost surely uniformly bounded by
(A7)
(a) and
(A8)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left[P_{0}\left|h\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}\xi_{\infty}\right|^{k}\right]^{1/k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left[\int\left|h\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(a)\right|^{k}\,dF_{0}(a)\right]^{1/k}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\{\int\left|he_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}w_{h,a_{0},1}(a)K_{h,a_{0}}(a)\right|^{k}\,dF_{0}(a)\right\}^{1/k}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left\{\int\left|hc_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}w_{b,a_{0},2}(a)K_{b,a_{0}}(a)\right|^{k}\,dF_{0}(a)\right\}^{1/k}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{1/k}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\mathbf{1}\right|+h^{1/k}\tau_{n}^{-1/k}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbf{1}\right|

By Lemma
4
and
(A8)
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}|
and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|c_{0,h,a_{0},2}\tau_{n}^{2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}|
are both
O(1)
. Therefore, the first term on the right hand side of (
16
) is bounded up to a constant by
h^{1/k}
as
h\longrightarrow 0
. The second term on the right hand side of (
16
) can similarly be shown to be bounded up to a constant by
h^{1/k}
using Lemma
4
and the fact that
\mu_{0}
is uniformly bounded since
|Y|
is
P_{0}
-almost surely uniformly bounded by
(A8)
.

Finally, the last term of (
16
) can be bounded using Jensen’s inequality as follows:

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\int\left|h\int\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\left\{\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},w)-\smallint\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},\bar{w})\,dQ_{0}(\bar{w})\right\}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\right|^{k}\,dQ_{0}(w)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\iint\left|h\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\left\{\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},w)-\int\mu_{\infty}(\bar{a},\bar{w})\,dQ_{0}(\bar{w})\right\}\right|^{k}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a})\,dQ_{0}(w)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\lesssim\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\int\left|h\Gamma_{0,h,b,a_{0}}(\bar{a})\right|^{k}\,dF_{0}(\bar{a}),

where the final inequality follows from the uniform bound on
\mu_{\infty}
assumed by
(A7)
(a). The final integral is bounded up to a constant by
h
as shown above.
∎

Lemma 24
.

Let
\{Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0}):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
be the mean-zero Gaussian process with covariance function

\Sigma_{\infty,h,b}:(u,v)\mapsto hP_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v})/[\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)].

If
(A1)
–
(A2)
,
(A7)
(a), and
(A8)
hold, then
\{Z_{\infty,h,b}:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
is tight in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
,
E_{0}\left[\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|\right]\leq C\sqrt{\log(1/h)}
for a constant
C
not depending on
h
, and

E\left[\sup_{|u-v|<\delta}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(u)-Z_{\infty,h,b}(v)\right|\right]\leq C^{\prime}h^{-1/2}([2\delta]\wedge h)^{1/2}\left[\log\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{0}|}{2([2\delta]\wedge h)}\right]^{1/2}

for all
[2\delta]\wedge h\leq|\mathcal{A}_{0}|/8
and a constant
C^{\prime}
not depending on
h
.

Proof of Lemma
24
.

Let
\rho_{\infty,h,b}
be the standard deviation semi-metric corresponding to
Z_{\infty,h,b}
; i.e.

\rho_{\infty,h,b}^{2}(u,v):=E\left[Z_{\infty,h,b}(u)-Z_{\infty,h,b}(v)\right]^{2}=|\Sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u,u)-2\Sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)+\Sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v,v)|=2\left|1-\Sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)\right|

because
\Sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u,u)=1
for any
u
by definition. We first establish that
\rho_{\infty,h,b}
is Lipschitz in
|u-v|
. By Lemma
22
, there exist constants
c,C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
h
small enough and all
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
,
c\leq\sigma^{2}_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\leq C
.
We can then bound
|1-\Sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)|
as follows:

\displaystyle|1-\Sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)|

\displaystyle=\left|1-hP_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v})/[\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)]\right|

\displaystyle=\left|1-\frac{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)}-\frac{hP_{0}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v}-\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u})}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)-\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)\right|}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)}+\frac{h\left|P_{0}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v}-\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u})\right|}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)-\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)\right|+h^{1/2}\left[P_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v})^{2}\right]^{1/2}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)}

\displaystyle\leq c^{-1/2}\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)-\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)\right|+c^{-1/2}h^{1/2}\left[P_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v})^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

Since
\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)
and
\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)
are bounded away from zero for all
h
small enough and
x\mapsto x^{1/2}
is Lipschitz on
[\varepsilon,\infty)
for any
\varepsilon>0
, we also have

\displaystyle\left|\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)-\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left[hP_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u})^{2}\right]^{1/2}-\left[hP_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v})^{2}\right]^{1/2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim h\left|P_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u})^{2}-P_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v})^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=h\left|P_{0}\left(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v}\right)\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}+P_{0}\left(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v}\right)\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v}\right|

\displaystyle\leq h^{1/2}\left[P_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v})^{2}\{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)^{2}+\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)^{2}\}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{1/2}\left[P_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v})^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

Hence, we can turn our attention to bounding
h^{1/2}[P_{0}(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v})^{2}]^{1/2}
. We have

\displaystyle h^{1/2}\left[P_{0}\left(\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,u}-\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,v}\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq h^{1/2}\left[P_{0}\{(\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,v})^{2}\xi_{\infty}^{2}\}\right]^{1/2}+h^{1/2}\left[P_{0}(\gamma_{0,h,b,u}-\gamma_{0,h,b,v})^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+h^{1/2}\left[P_{0}\left(\int\left\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,v}\right\}\left\{\mu_{\infty}-\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right\}\,dF_{0}\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

For the first term in the display, since
|\xi_{\infty}|
is
P_{0}
-almost surely uniformly bounded, we have

\displaystyle\left[P_{0}\{(\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,v})^{2}\xi_{\infty}^{2}\}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\lesssim\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}w_{h,u,1}(a)K_{h,u}(a)-e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}w_{h,v,1}(a)K_{h,v}(a)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\tau_{n}^{2}\left[\int\left\{c_{0,h,u,2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,u,2}^{-1}w_{b,u,2}(a)K_{b,u}(a)-c_{0,h,v,2}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,v,2}^{-1}w_{b,v,2}(a)K_{b,v}(a)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}.

(17)

For the first term, we note that since the support of
K
is contained in
[-1,1]
, if
|u-v|>2h
, then either
K_{h,u}(a)=0
or
K_{h,v}(a)=0
for all
a
. Hence, if
|u-v|>2h
, then

\displaystyle\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}w_{h,u,1}(a)K_{h,u}(a)-e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}w_{h,v,1}(a)K_{h,v}(a)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}w_{h,u,1}(a)K_{h,u}(a)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da+\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v}^{-1}w_{h,v,1}(a)K_{h,v}(a)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=h^{-1/2}\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}(1,t)K(t)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(u+th)\,dt+\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}(1,t)K(t)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(v+th)\,dt\right]^{1/2},

which is bounded up to a constant by
h^{-1/2}
by the boundedness of
K
,
f_{0}
, and Lemma
4
. If
|u-v|\leq 2h
, then we further decompose

\displaystyle\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}w_{h,u,1}(a)K_{h,u}(a)-e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}w_{h,v,1}(a)K_{h,v}(a)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}\left[w_{h,u,1}(a)-w_{h,v,1}(a)\right]\right\}^{2}K_{h,u}(a)^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\left[\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}\right]w_{h,v,1}(a)\right\}^{2}K_{h,u}(a)^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}w_{h,v,1}(a)\right\}^{2}\left\{K_{h,u}(a)-K_{h,v}(a)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}.

For the first term, we have
w_{h,u,1}(a)-w_{h,v,1}(a)=(0,(v-u)/h)
, so
e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}\left[w_{h,u,1}(a)-w_{h,v,1}(a)\right]=h^{-1}(v-u)\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}[1,2]=(v-u)O(1)
since
\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}=f_{0}(u)^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{2}^{-1}+O(h)
by Lemma
4
and
\mathbf{S}_{2}
is a diagonal matrix. Hence, the first term is bounded up to a constant by

\displaystyle|u-v|\left[\int\left\{K_{h,u}(a)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=h^{-1/2}|u-v|\left[\int\left\{K(t)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(u+th)\,dt\right]^{1/2},

which is bounded up to a constant by
h^{-1/2}|u-v|
for all
h
small enough.

For the second term, we can write
\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}=\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}(\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1})\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}
, and we have by definition

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left(w_{h,v,1}w_{h,v,1}^{T}K_{h,v}-w_{h,u,1}w_{h,u,1}^{T}K_{h,u}\right)

\displaystyle=P_{0}\left(\left[w_{h,v,1}w_{h,v,1}^{T}-w_{h,u,1}w_{h,u,1}^{T}\right]K_{h,v}+w_{h,u,1}w_{h,u,1}^{T}\left[K_{h,v}-K_{h,u}\right]\right).

We then note that

w_{h,v,1}(a)w_{h,v,1}(a)^{T}-w_{h,u,1}(a)w_{h,u,1}(a)^{T}=h^{-1}(v-u)\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&[2a-u-v]/h\end{pmatrix}.

With the change of variables
t=(a-v)/h
, we then have

\displaystyle\left|P_{0}\left(\left[w_{h,v,1}w_{h,v,1}^{T}-w_{h,u,1}w_{h,u,1}^{T}\right]K_{h,v}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=h^{-1}|u-v|\left|\int\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&2t+(v-u)/h\end{pmatrix}K(t)f_{0}(v+th)\,dt\right|.

Since we are considering the case
|u-v|\leq 2h
, the absolute value of this expression is bounded up to a constant by
h^{-1}|u-v|
. We also have

\displaystyle\left|P_{0}\left(w_{h,u,1}w_{h,u,1}^{T}\left[K_{h,v}-K_{h,u}\right]\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int(1,t)(1,t)^{T}\left[K(t)-K(t+[u-v]/h)\right]f_{0}(u+th)\,dt\right|,

which is bounded up to a constant by
h^{-1}|u-v|
by the Lipschitz assumption on
K
. Hence,

\displaystyle\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\left[\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}-\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}\right]w_{h,v,1}(a)\right\}^{2}K_{h,u}(a)^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq Ch^{-1}|u-v|\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}w_{h,v,1}(a)\right\}^{2}K_{h,u}(a)^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=Ch^{-3/2}|u-v|\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}(1,t)\right\}^{2}K(t+(v-u)/h)f_{0}(v+th)\,da\right]^{1/2},

which is bounded up to a constant by
h^{-3/2}|u-v|
.

For the final term, since
K
is assumed to be Lipschitz, we have

\displaystyle\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}w_{h,v,1}(a)\left[K_{h,u}(a)-K_{h,v}(a)\right]\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\lesssim\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}w_{h,v,1}(a)\right\}^{2}h^{-2}\left[(a-u)/h-(a-v)/h\right]^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt=h^{-3/2}|u-v|\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}(1,t)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(u+th)\,dt\right]^{1/2},

which is bounded up to a constant by
h^{-3/2}|u-v|
.

Putting it together, we have that

h^{1/2}\left[\int\left\{e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,u,1}^{-1}w_{h,u,1}(a)K_{h,u}(a)-e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,v,1}^{-1}w_{h,v,1}(a)K_{h,v}(a)\right\}^{2}f_{0}(a)\,da\right]^{1/2}

is bounded up to a constant by
h^{-3/2}|u-v|
when
|u-v|\leq 2h
and is bounded up to a constant when
|u-v|>2h
. Analysis of the second term of (
17
) follows the same logic, and yields the same result. We can also show using the above techniques that
h^{1/2}\left[P_{0}(\gamma_{0,h,b,u}-\gamma_{0,h,b,v})^{2}\right]^{1/2}
satisfies the same bound. Finally, since
\mu_{\infty}
is uniformly bounded, we have

\displaystyle\left[P_{0}\left(\int\left\{\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,v}\right\}\left\{\mu_{\infty}-\int\mu_{\infty}\,dQ_{0}\right\}\,dF_{0}\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int\left|\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,v}\right|\,dF_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\int\left(\Gamma_{0,h,b,u}-\Gamma_{0,h,b,v}\right)^{2}\,dF_{0}\right]^{1/2},

which is the same as the expression we bounded above.

We have now shown that there exist a constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
not depending on
h
,
u
, or
v
such that
\rho_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)\leq C_{1}h^{-1/2}|u-v|^{1/2}
for
|u-v|\leq 2h
, and
\rho_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)\leq C_{2}
for
|u-v|>2h
. Without loss of generality, we can take
C_{2}=\sqrt{2}C_{1}
so the bound is continuous. Suppose
\varepsilon\in(0,C_{1}/\sqrt{2}]
and
|u-v|\leq 4C_{1}^{-2}h\varepsilon^{2}
. Then
|u-v|\leq 2h
, so

\rho_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)\leq C_{1}h^{-1/2}|u-v|^{1/2}\leq 2\varepsilon.

Hence, for
\varepsilon\leq C_{1}/\sqrt{2}
,
N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{0},\rho_{\infty,h,b})\leq C_{1}^{2}|\mathcal{A}_{0}|/(4h\varepsilon^{2})
. For
\varepsilon>C_{1}/\sqrt{2}
and
|u-v|\leq 2h
, we have
\rho_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)\leq C_{1}h^{-1/2}|u-v|^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{2}C_{1}<2\varepsilon
. For
\varepsilon>C_{1}/\sqrt{2}
and
|u-v|>2h
, we have
\rho_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)\leq\sqrt{2}C_{1}<2\varepsilon
. Therefore, all
u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
fit in a single
\rho_{\infty,h,b}
ball of radius
\varepsilon
, so
N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{0},\rho_{\infty,h,b})=1
for
\varepsilon>C_{1}/\sqrt{2}
.

This implies that
\mathcal{A}_{0}
can be covered by finitely many
\rho_{\infty,h,b}
balls of radius
\varepsilon
for every
\varepsilon>0
. Hence, the semimetric space
(\mathcal{A}_{0},\rho_{\infty,h,b})
is totally bounded, and hence separable (see, e.g. page 17 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
). Furthermore, since
Z_{\infty,h,b}
is a Gaussian process, it is sub-Gaussian with respect to its intrinsic semimetric
\rho_{\infty,h,b}
. We thus conclude
\{Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0}):a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
is a separable sub-Gaussian process with respect to
\rho_{\infty,h,b}
. We then have by Corollary 2.2.8 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
that

\displaystyle E\left[\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim E\left[|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{1})|\right]+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\{\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{0},\rho_{\infty,h,b})\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq 1+\int_{0}^{C_{1}/\sqrt{2}}\left\{\log\left(C_{1}^{2}|\mathcal{A}_{0}|/[4h\varepsilon^{2}]\right)\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{C_{1}\left[1+\log\left(|\mathcal{A}_{0}|/[2h]\right)\right]}{2\left[\log\left(|\mathcal{A}_{0}|/[2h]\right)\right]^{1/2}},

which is bounded up to a constant by
\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{1/2}
for all
h
small enough. For every
\delta\in(0,C_{1}/\sqrt{2})
, we also have by Corollary 2.2.8 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
that

\displaystyle E\left[\sup_{\rho_{\infty,h,b}(s,t)<\delta}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{1})-Z_{\infty,h,b}(t)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{\delta}\left\{\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{0},\rho_{\infty,h,b})\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon.

Since the integral is finite over
[0,\infty)
(as shown above), the integral over
[0,\delta]
goes to zero as
\delta
goes to zero. Hence, the sample paths of
Z_{\infty,h,b}
are almost surely uniformly
\rho_{\infty,h,b}
-continuous. Since
(\mathcal{A}_{0},\rho_{\infty,h,b})
is totally bounded, this implies that
Z_{\infty,h,b}
is tight in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{A}_{0})
.

Finally, since
\rho_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)\leq C_{1}h^{-1/2}(\min\{|u-v|,2h\})^{1/2}
, we have again by Corollary 2.2.8 of
van der Vaart and Wellner, 1996
that

\displaystyle E\left[\sup_{|u-v|<\delta}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(u)-Z_{\infty,h,b}(v)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq E\left[\sup_{\rho_{\infty,h,b}(u,v)<C_{1}h^{-1/2}(\delta\wedge[2h])^{1/2}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(u)-Z_{\infty,h,b}(v)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{C_{1}h^{-1/2}(\delta\wedge[2h])^{1/2}}\left\{\log N(\varepsilon,\mathcal{A}_{0},\rho_{\infty,h,b})\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{C_{1}h^{-1/2}(\delta\wedge[h/2])^{1/2}}\left\{\log\left(C_{1}^{2}|\mathcal{A}_{0}|/[4h\varepsilon^{2}]\right)\right\}^{1/2}\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{-1/2}([2\delta]\wedge h)^{1/2}\left[\log\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{0}|}{2([2\delta]\wedge h)}\right]^{1/2}

as long as
[2\delta]\wedge h\leq|\mathcal{A}_{0}|/8
.
∎

Lemma 25
.

If
(A1)
–
(A2)
,
(A7)
(a), and
(A8)
hold, then

\displaystyle\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|-\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|=O_{p}\left(\{nh\}^{-1/2}\{\log n\}^{3/2}+\{nh\}^{-1/4}\{\log n\}^{5/4}+\{nh\}^{-1/6}\log n\right).

Hence, if in addition
nh^{p}\longrightarrow\infty
for some
p>1
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|\leq t\right)-P\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)\right|=o(1).

Proof of Lemma
25
.

These results are an application of Corollary 2.2 and Lemma 2.4 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
. For each
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
and
h,b>0
, we consider the following function:

\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}:=(y,a,w)\mapsto h^{1/2}\sigma^{-1}_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}(y,a,w).

We then define the class of functions
\mathcal{H}_{h,b}:=\{\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}:a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\}
. In the notation of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
, we have
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{H}_{h,b}\cup-\mathcal{H}_{h,b}
,

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{G}_{n}f=\sup_{\eta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,b}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\eta\right|=\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|,

and
\tilde{Z}=\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|
, where by definition,
Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})
is a mean-zero Gaussian process on
\mathcal{A}_{0}
with covariance function
(u,v)\mapsto P_{0}(\eta_{h,b,u}\eta_{h,b,v})
.

We now verify the conditions of Corollary 2.2 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
. First,
\mathcal{H}_{h,b}
is pointwise measurable because
K
is uniformly continuous by
(A1)
, and by Corollary
5
,
\mathcal{H}_{h,b}
is VC type. For each
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
and
h,b>0
, we have
P_{0}\eta_{h,b,a_{0}}^{2}=1
by definition of
\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})
. By Lemmas
22
and
23
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in\mathcal{H}_{h,b}}P_{0}|\eta|^{3}=\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\frac{P_{0}\left|h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}\right|^{3}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})^{3}}\lesssim h^{-1/2}

Hence, in the notation of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
, we have
\sigma^{2}=1
and
b=h^{-1/2}
up to a constant not depending on
h
. We next establish that
\mathcal{H}_{h,b}
is uniformly bounded up to a constant by
h^{-1/2}
. We let
\mathbf{1}
be a vector of 1’s of the appropriate dimension. Using the boundedness and bounded support of
K
as well as the uniform boundedness of
\mu_{\infty}
, for each
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left|h\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}(y,a,w)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\mathbf{1}+c_{0,h,a_{0},2}(h/b)^{3}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbf{1}\right|\left\{\left|\xi_{\infty}(y,a,w)\right|+2K_{0}\right\}\|K\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left|e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}^{-1}_{0,h,a_{0},1}\right|\left|P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|\|K\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left|c_{0,h,a_{0},2}(h/b)^{3}e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}\right|\left|P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|\|K\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+(h/b)^{2}\left|e_{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}\right|\left|\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}P_{0}(\tilde{w}_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}})\right|\|K\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\times\left|e_{3}^{T}\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|

By Lemma
4
, the elements of
\mathbf{D}_{0,h,a_{0},1}^{-1}
and
\mathbf{D}_{0,b,a_{0},2}^{-1}
are uniformly bounded over
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
and for all
h
small enough. By the uniform boundedness of
\theta_{0}
and
f_{0}
in an enlargement of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
, we can also show using a change of variables that
\left|P_{0}\left(w_{h,a_{0},1}K_{h,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|
and
\left|P_{0}\left(w_{b,a_{0},2}K_{b,a_{0}}\theta_{0}\right)\right|
are uniformly bounded for all
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
and
h
small enough. Hence, there are finite positive constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
not depending on
h
,
a_{0}
, or
(y,a,w)
such that
\left|h\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}(y,a,w)\right|\leq C_{1}+C_{2}|\xi_{\infty}(y,a,w)|
for all
(y,a,w)
,
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
and
h
small enough. Therefore, by Lemma
22
, an envelope function for
\mathcal{H}_{h,b}
is given by
h^{-1/2}(C_{1}^{\prime}+C_{2}^{\prime}|\xi_{\infty}|)
for finite positive constants
C_{1}^{\prime}
and
C_{2}^{\prime}
. By
(A7)
(a) and
(A8)
,
|\xi_{\infty}|
is uniformly bounded. Thus,
\mathcal{H}_{h,b}
is uniformly bounded up to a constant by
b=h^{-1/2}
, so the moment and envelope conditions of Corollary 2.2 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
hold.

We have now checked all the conditions of Corollary 2.2 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
, so with
\gamma_{n}=(\log n)^{-1}
, it follows that for all
h
small enough and a constant
C
not depending on
h
,

\displaystyle P\left(\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}h^{1/2}\frac{\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|-\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|{Z}_{\infty,h,b}(s)\right|\right|>\frac{Ch^{-1/2}\{\log n\}^{3/2}}{n^{1/2}}+\frac{Ch^{-1/4}\{\log n\}^{5/4}}{n^{1/4}}+\frac{Ch^{-1/6}\log n}{n^{1/6}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\lesssim\frac{1}{\log n}+\frac{\log n}{n}

We conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{\phi^{*}_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|-\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\right|=O_{p}\left(r_{n}\right)

for

r_{n}=\{nh\}^{-1/2}\{\log n\}^{3/2}+\{nh\}^{-1/4}\{\log n\}^{5/4}+\{nh\}^{-1/6}\log n.

For the second statement, we use Lemma 2.4 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
. In their notation, we have
\mathcal{F}_{n}=(h^{-1/2}\mathcal{H}_{h,b})\cup(-h^{-1/2}\mathcal{H}_{h,b})
. We have already established that this class is pointwise measurable, that its envelope is square integrable, and that its variance function is uniformly bounded above and below for all
n
. Lemma
24
implies that
\mathcal{F}_{n}
is
P_{0}
-pre-Gaussian. For the final condition of Lemma 2.4 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
, by Lemma
24
, we have
E_{0}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|=O\left(\{\log(1/h)\}^{1/2}\right)
. The assumption that
nh^{p}\longrightarrow\infty
for some
p>1
then implies that
r_{n}\{\log(1/h)\}^{1/2}=o(1)
, which verifies the last condition of Lemma 2.4 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
.

∎

Lemma 26
.

If the conditions of Lemma
24
hold, then for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P\left(\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|-t\right|\leq\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2}\right)\leq C\varepsilon+o(1)

as
h\longrightarrow 0
for
C
not depending on
h
or
\varepsilon
.

Proof of Lemma
26
.

We use Lemma A.1 from the supplementary material of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
. We define the class of functions
\mathcal{H}_{h,b}:=\{\phi_{\infty,h,b,s}^{*}/\sigma_{\infty,h,b,}(s):s\in\mathcal{S}\}
, and in the notation of Lemma A.1 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
, we set
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{H}_{h,b}\cup-\mathcal{H}_{h,b}
. By Lemma
24
, a tight Gaussian process in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{F})
exists, so
\mathcal{F}
is
P_{0}
-pre-Gaussian, and by definition,
\mathrm{Var}_{0}(f)=1
for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
. Hence, the conditions of Lemma A.1 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
are satisfied, and we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P\left(\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|-t\right|\leq\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq C\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2}\left[E_{0}\left\{\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\right\}+\left\{\log\left(\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{1/2}/\varepsilon\right)\right\}^{1/2}\right]

for
C
not depending on
h
or
\varepsilon
. By Lemma
24
,
E_{0}\left\{\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,bo}(s)|\right\}=O(\{\log h^{-1}\}^{1/2})
. Furthermore,

\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2}\left\{\log\left(\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{1/2}/\varepsilon\right)\right\}^{1/2}=o(1)

as
h\longrightarrow 0
for any
\varepsilon>0
. The result follows.
∎

Lemma 27
.

If
(A1)
–
(A3)
and
(A6)
–
(A8)
hold, then

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}(nh)^{1/2}\left|\frac{\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})}{\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|\leq t\right)-P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)\right|=o(1).

Proof of Lemma
27
.

Since
(A7)
implies that
(A4)
holds for all
a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
, by Lemma
3
,

\displaystyle P\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}(nh)^{1/2}\left|\frac{\theta_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\theta_{0}(a_{0})}{\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|\leq t\right)

\displaystyle=P\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}(a_{0})+R_{n}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right),

where we define

R_{n}(a_{0}):=(nh)^{1/2}\sum_{j=1}^{6}R_{n,h,b,a_{0},j}/\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})+\frac{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})-\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})}{\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})}\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}

and
G_{n}(a_{0}):=\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}
. By the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}\right|\leq t-\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}+R_{n}\right|\leq t\right)\leq P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}\right|\leq t+\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)

for each
t\in\mathbb{R}
. We then have

\displaystyle P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}+R_{n}\right|\leq t\right)-P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}\right|\leq t+\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)-P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t+\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t+\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)-P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\left|P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}\right|\leq t+\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)-P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t+\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)\right|+P\left(t<\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t+\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}\right|\leq t\right)-P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)\right|+P\left(\left|\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|-t\right|\leq\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right).

Similarly,

\displaystyle P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}+R_{n}\right|\leq t\right)-P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}\right|\leq t-\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)-P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t-\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t-\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)-P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq-\left|P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}\right|\leq t-\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)-P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t-\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right)\right|-P\left(t-\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|<\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\geq-\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|G_{n}\right|\leq t\right)-P\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)\right|-P\left(\left|\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|-t\right|\leq\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}|\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}(nh)^{1/2}\left|\frac{\theta_{n,h,b}(s)-\theta_{0}(s)}{\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|\leq t\right)-P\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|\leq t\right)-P\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P\left(\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|-t\right|\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}(a_{0})|\right)

The first term on the right hand side is
o(1)
by Lemma
25
. For the second term, for any
\varepsilon>0
, we can write

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P\left(\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|-t\right|\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}(a_{0})|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P\left(\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|-t\right|\leq\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+P\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}(a_{0})|>\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2}\right).

(18)

By Lemma
26
, the first term is bounded by
C\varepsilon+o(1)
for
C
not depending on
\varepsilon
or
h
. For the second term, since
nh\longrightarrow\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\log h^{-1}\right)^{1/2}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,a_{0}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})^{-1}\right]\left[\sum_{j=1}^{6}(nh\log n)^{1/2}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},j}|\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left.(\log n)^{1/2}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|\right].

By Lemmas
22
and
21
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})^{-1}=O_{p}(1)
. By Lemma
7
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},1}|=O(h^{2+\delta_{4}})
for some
\delta_{4}>0
. Since
nh^{5}=O(1)
, we then have
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},1}|=o(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2})
. By Corollary
6
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}|
and
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},2}|
are
o_{p}(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2})
as long as
nh^{p}=o(1)
for some
p>0
, which holds for
p=6
since
nh^{5}=O(1)
and
h=o(1)
. By Lemma
17
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},4}|=o_{p}(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2})
. By Lemma
19
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},5}|=O_{p}(\{nh\}^{-1})=o_{p}(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2})
since
nh/\log h^{-1}
and
nh/\log n
both go to
\infty
by
(A6)
. Finally, by Lemma
20
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},6}|=O_{p}(\{nh/\log h^{-1}\}^{-1})=o_{p}(\{nh\log n\}^{-1/2})
by the same logic. Hence,
\sum_{j=1}^{6}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,h,b,a_{0},j}|=o_{p}(\{nh\log n\}^{1/2})
.

By Lemmas
24
and
25
,

\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{G}_{n}\frac{h^{1/2}\phi_{\infty,h,b,a_{0}}^{*}}{\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})}\right|=O(\{\log n\}^{1/2})+o_{p}(1),

and by Lemma
21
,
\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|\sigma_{n,h,b}(a_{0})-\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})|=o_{p}(\{\log n\}^{-1})
.

We have now shown that
\left(\log h^{-1}\right)^{1/2}\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n,a_{0}}|=o_{p}(1)
, which implies that the second term on the right hand side of (
18
) goes to zero for any
\varepsilon>0
. Since
\varepsilon
was arbitrary, this implies that

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P\left(\left|\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|-t\right|\leq\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}|R_{n}(a_{0})|\right)=o(1),

which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 28
.

If the conditions of Lemma
24
hold and
\omega_{n}=o\left(h^{p}\right)
for some
p>1
, then

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left(\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)-P_{0}\left(\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)\right|=o(1).

Proof of Lemma
28
.

We write

\displaystyle\left|P_{0}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)-P_{0}\left(\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|E_{0}\left[I\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)-I\left(\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq E_{0}\left[\left|I\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)-I\left(\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t,\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|>t\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+P_{0}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|>t,\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right).

We address the two probabilities in the final expression in the same way, so we only provide the derivation for the first term. For any
\varepsilon>0
, we can write

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P_{0}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t,\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|>t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P_{0}\left(\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t-\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2},\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|>t\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P_{0}\left(t-\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2}<\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\leq t\right)

\displaystyle\leq P_{0}\left(\left|\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|-\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\right|>\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}P_{0}\left(\left|\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|-t\right|<\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2}\right).

By Lemma
26
, the second term in the last inequality is bounded by
C\varepsilon+o(1)
. For the first term, we note that by the definition of the mesh
\omega_{n}
of
\mathcal{A}_{n}
,

\left|\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}|-\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}|Z_{\infty,h,b}|\right|\leq\sup_{|u-v|\leq\omega_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(u)-Z_{\infty,h,b}(v)\right|

almost surely. By Markov’s inequality and Lemma
24
, we then have

\displaystyle P_{0}\left(\left|\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|-\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\right|>\varepsilon\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{1/2}E_{0}\left[\left|\sup_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|-\max_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}\right|\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{1/2}E_{0}\left[\sup_{|u-v|\leq\omega_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(u)-Z_{\infty,h,b}(v)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}\left[\log h^{-1}\right]^{1/2}h^{-1/2}([2\omega_{n}]\wedge h)^{1/2}\left[\log\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{0}|}{2([2\omega_{n}]\wedge h)}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-1}\left[\log h^{-1}([2\omega_{n}/h]\wedge 1)\log\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{0}|}{2([2\omega_{n}]\wedge h)}\right]^{1/2}.

Now
\omega_{n}=o\left(h^{p}\right)
implies that
\omega_{n}/h\longrightarrow 0
, so for all
n
large enough the above expression is equal to

\varepsilon^{-1}\left[h^{-1}\log h^{-1}\omega_{n}\log\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{0}|}{4\omega_{n}}\right]^{1/2}.

This goes to zero by the assumed rate for
\omega_{n}
for every
\varepsilon>0
.
∎

Lemma 29
.

If
(A1)
–
(A3)
and
(A6)
–
(A8)
,
nh^{5}=O(1)
, and
mn^{d}=O(1)
for some
d\in(0,\infty)
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left(\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)-P_{0}\left(\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{n,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\mid\mathbf{O}_{n}\right)\right|=o_{p}(1).

Proof of Lemma
29
.

Given
O_{1},\dotsc,O_{n}
,
Z_{n,h,b}
is a Gaussian process on
\mathcal{A}_{n}
. Hence, we can use the Gaussian comparison result of Theorem 2 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{n}:=\{a_{1}<a_{2}<\cdots<a_{m}\}
. In their notation, we then have
Y_{j}=Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{j})
and
X_{j}=Z_{n,h,b}(a_{j})
for
1\leq j\leq m
, and
Y_{j}=-Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{j})
and
X_{j}=-Z_{n,h,b}(a_{j})
for
1+m\leq j\leq 2m
. Hence,
\max_{1\leq j\leq 2m}Y_{j}=\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|
and
\max_{1\leq j\leq 2m}X_{j}=\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{n,h,b}(a_{0})\right|
. Hence, we have
p=2m
. Since
Z_{\infty,h,b}
and
Z_{n,h,b}
are both normalized, we have
\sigma_{jj}^{X}=\sigma_{jj}^{Y}=1
for all
j
. By Lemma
24
, we have

a_{p}\leq E\left[\sup_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\right]=O\left(\{\log h^{-1}\}^{1/2}\right).

We define

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{0}}\left|\mathbb{P}_{n}(h\phi_{n,h,b,u}^{*}\phi_{n,h,b,v}^{*})/[\sigma_{n,h,b}(u)\sigma_{n,h,b}(v)]-P_{0}(h\phi_{\infty,h,b,u}^{*}\phi_{\infty,h,b,v}^{*})/[\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(u)\sigma_{\infty,h,b}(v)]\right|.

Then by Theorem 2 of
Chernozhukov et al., 2014
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|P_{0}\left(\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{\infty,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\right)-P_{0}\left(\max_{a_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\left|Z_{n,h,b}(a_{0})\right|\leq t\mid\mathbf{O}_{n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left[\Delta_{n}\{\log(2m)\}\log\left(h^{-1}\vee\Delta_{n}^{-1}\right)\right]^{1/3}.

By Lemmas
21
and
22
,
\Delta_{n}=o_{p}(n^{-p})
for some
p>0
. Since
\log m\lesssim\log n
by assumption, the right hand side of the preceding display is
o_{p}(1)
.

∎

Supplement N
Additional results from numerical studies

This section presents additional results from the numerical study described in Section 4 of the main text.
In addition to data adaptive nuisance estimators, we consider the following parametric estimators. First, we estimated
\mu_{0}
using a correctly specified logistic regression model to obtain estimators of
\gamma_{1}
,
\gamma_{2}
,
\gamma_{3}
and
\gamma_{4}
. We estimated
g_{0}
using maximum likelihood estimation with a correctly specified parametric model to obtain an estimator of
\beta
. To investigate the double-robustness of our estimator, we also estimated
\mu_{0}
and
g_{0}
using incorrectly specified parametric models. For
\mu_{0}
, we used the incorrectly specified logistic regression model that assumes
\mu_{0}(a,w)=\mathrm{expit}\left(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{T}\tilde{w}+\tilde{\gamma}_{2}a\right)
for some
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
\tilde{\gamma}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, where
\tilde{w}:=(1,w_{1},w_{2})
. For
g_{0}
, we used the incorrectly specified linear regression model that assumes that given
W=w
,
A
follows a normal distribution with mean
\tilde{\gamma_{3}}^{T}\tilde{w}
for some
\tilde{\gamma}_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and constant variance
.

Figures
7
and
8
display
the pointwise empirical bias and variance for sample sizes
n=500,1000
, and
2500
.
Figure
7
corresponds to the nuisance parameter estimation based on parametric models and Figure
8
corresponds to the nuisance parameter estimation based on SuperLearner.
The squared bias of the local linear estimators remains large for
n=2500

unless undersmoothing is employed
. The variance of the local linear estimator, on the other hand, is smaller than that of the debiased estimator compared with the corresponding bandwidth selection procedure.
The variance of undersmoothed local linear estimator is comparable with the debiased estimator using LOOCV, and it increases faster than the optimal rate
n^{4/5}
.
The variance of all estimators is generally larger when the outcome regression model is misspecified.
The conclusion is similar when using parametric and SuperLearner-based nuisance estimators.

Figure
9
displays the pointwise empirical mean squared error (MSE) of the estimators for
n=500
,
1000
and
2500
.
The results include both parametric model-based and SuperLearner-based nuisance estimators.
For all sample sizes, the debiased local linear estimator with the plug-in bandwidth selection procedure attains the smallest MSE for most interior points we considered. The local linear estimator using plug-in bandwidth selection attains a small MSE at points where the second derivative of
\theta_{0}
is close to zero, but for points where the second derivative is far from zero, it has a larger MSE due to its bias. For the debiased estimator, the LOOCV bandwidth selection yields similar MSEs regardless of optimizing over both
b
and
h
or just over
h
with
b=h
fixed.
When
n=500
, the undersmoothed local linear estimator displays a slightly smaller MSE than the debiased estimator with LOOCV bandwidth selection; however, as
n
increases, the MSE grows, indicating suboptimal convergence rate of MSE when undersmoothing is employed. The undersmoothing technique consistently yields larger MSEs compared to the debiased local linear estimator with the plug-in bandwidth selection for all interior points and sample sizes considered. All estimators display larger MSE towards the boundary points.

Figure
10
displays the empirical coverage of pointwise 95% confidence intervals for sample sizes
n=500,1000
and
n=2500
. The coverage of confidence intervals based on the local linear estimator does not improve when the sample size is larger. On the other hand, the coverage of the confidence intervals based on the debiased estimator are slightly lower for
n=500
, and generally very close to 95% when
n=2500
. The coverage accuracy is particularly good when at least one of the nuisance estimators is based on a correctly specified parametric model. On the other hand, we observe slightly worse coverage near the boundary when only conditional density is correctly specified and is based on SuperLearner.

Figure
11
displays the median length of pointwise 95% confidence intervals for two sample sizes,
n=500,1000
and
n=2500
. We observe that the widths of the confidence intervals are generally comparable between local linear estimators and the debiased estimators when the plug-in method is used, indicating that the bias correction has a relative minor impact on confidence interval widths. When undersmoothing is used, the confidence intervals widen and align with the debiased local linear estimators obtained through LOOCV bandwidth selection procedures. Comparatively, the confidence intervals based on the debiased local linear estimator with the plug-in method exhibit narrower widths than those of the local linear estimator with undersmoothing. This gap widens as the sample size
n
increases, as undersmoothing results in a suboptimal convergence rate.

Figures
12

and
13
display
the empirical coverage of confidence intervals for the causal effect
\theta_{0}(a)-\theta_{0}(0.5)
based for sample sizes
n=500,1000
and
n=2500
. The conclusion is similar to the case with
n=1000
from the main text; the confidence intervals based on the asymptotic independence are conservative when the distance between the evaluation points is small. This is still a problem at
n=2500
. The influence function-based confidence intervals perform particularly well
when nuisance function is estimated based on parametric models as seen in Figure
12
. The conclusion for SuperLearner-based methods are similar except that the influence function-based confidence intervals based on the plug-in bandwidth selection overcover when the outcome regression is misspecified.

Figures
14
displays the empirical coverage of the uniform confidence bands over
\mathcal{A}_{0}=[0,1.0]
obtained from nuisance estimators based on parametric models and SuperLearner. The coverage accuracy of uniform bands does not show a significant difference between parametric and data-adaptive nuisance estimators. The plug-in bandwidth selection performs well for sample sizes greater than
1000
while methods based on cross-validation show slight undercoverage for large sample sizes.

Figure
15
displays distributions of the bandwidth
h
selected by the
five
procedures. We note that the bandwidth for the plug-in method is the same for the local linear and debiased estimators. The plug-in method generally selects larger bandwidths than the LOOCV methods. This explains the larger bias and smaller variance of the estimators based on the plug-in method. The distribution of
h
for the LOOCV method that selects both
h
and
b
has the largest variance. Figure
16
shows the densities of
\tau=h/b
for the LOOCV method that selects both
h
and
b
. Surprisingly, the procedure seems to favor smaller
\tau
than one. This explains the larger variance of the LOOCV method that selects both
h
and
b
relative to the LOOCV method that fixes
b=h
.

Finally, Figure
17
displays the true covariate-adjusted regression function used across the numerical study and its second derivative. The location that corresponds to a large second derivative in its absolute value coincides with the area where local linear estimators demonstrate larger bias and poor coverage.
